B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

KP的目标女孩中有82pc接种了HPV疫苗

82pc of KP’s targeted girls vaccinated against HPV
新闻简报In Khyber Pakhtunkhwa (KP), health workers have vaccinated over 82% of targeted girls aged 9-14 against human papillomavirus (HPV) during a two-week campaign aimed at preventing cervical cancer. The campaign, conducted in collaboration with the education department, WHO, Unicef, and Gavi, achieved the highest coverage in the country, with 2.281 million girls vaccinated out of a target of 2.843 million. Of the targeted girls, 620,978 remained unvaccinated, with 12 designated districts achieving 67.4% coverage and the remaining districts collectively reaching 84.8% coverage. Officials noted that concerns about vaccine safety, parental consent, and the purpose of vaccination were addressed through evidence-based information and dialogue. Safety monitoring mechanisms were in place, and no serious adverse events were recorded. To ensure full coverage, HPV vaccination services will continue at fixed centres from November 25 to 30, 2026.
查看新闻五要素
时间
开伯尔-普赫图赫瓦省 (KP) 的 HPV 疫苗接种 活动为期两周,于 2026 年 11 月 24 日星期三结束。证据中提到,疫苗接种 服务将在 2026 年 11 月 25 日至 30 日期间继续进行。
地点与对象
该活动在开伯尔-普赫图赫瓦省 (KP) 开展,特别是在 12 个指定地区和其余地区集中开展。目标人群是9-14岁的女孩。
事件
KP 的卫生工作者在 HPV 疫苗接种 运动期间为超过 82% 的目标女孩接种了疫苗,该运动旨在预防 宫颈癌。该活动是与教育部、世界卫生组织、联合国儿童基金会和全球疫苗和免疫联盟合作开展的。
规模、影响与风险
A total of 2.281 million girls were vaccinated out of a target of 2.843 million. Of the targeted girls, 620,978 remained unvaccinated. The campaign achieved 84.8% coverage in the remaining districts and 67.4% in the 12 designated districts. The World Health Organisation had requested KP and Balochistan authorities to improve public awareness.
应对、状态与不确定性
该活动在各利益相关者的参与下成功实施。安全监测机制到位,未发生严重不良事件。HPV 疫苗接种 服务将于 2026 年 11 月 25 日至 30 日继续在固定中心提供,以覆盖那些错过最初活动的人。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

82pc of KP’s targeted girls vaccinated against HPV

News Brief
In Khyber Pakhtunkhwa (KP), health workers have vaccinated over 82% of targeted girls aged 9-14 against human papillomavirus (HPV) during a two-week campaign aimed at preventing cervical cancer. The campaign, conducted in collaboration with the education department, WHO, Unicef, and Gavi, achieved the highest coverage in the country, with 2.281 million girls vaccinated out of a target of 2.843 million. Of the targeted girls, 620,978 remained unvaccinated, with 12 designated districts achieving 67.4% coverage and the remaining districts collectively reaching 84.8% coverage. Officials noted that concerns about vaccine safety, parental consent, and the purpose of vaccination were addressed through evidence-based information and dialogue. Safety monitoring mechanisms were in place, and no serious adverse events were recorded. To ensure full coverage, HPV vaccination services will continue at fixed centres from November 25 to 30, 2026.
Original source text (English)
EPAPER LIVE TV DAWNNEWS URDU Images Herald Aurora CityFM89 Advertise Events Supplements Careers Obituaries Dawn Epaper E-Paper | November 28, 2026 Dawn Epaper Home Latest Pakistan Opinion Business World Culture Prism Sport Magazines Tech Popular Archive Flood Donations Home Latest Pakistan Opinion Business World Culture Prism Sport Magazines Tech Popular Archive Flood Donations Search Search Page 29 · Metro - Peshawar Friday, 27 November 2026 82pc of KP’s targeted girls vaccinated against HPV By Ashfaq Yusufzai Newspaper Text A− A+ Email Story Your Name * Recipient Email * Send Email Cancel PESHAWAR: Health workers in Khyber Pakhtunkhwa have vaccinated more than 82pc of the targeted girls during the human papillomavirus (HPV) vaccination campaign, achieving the highest coverage in the country. The two-week exercise, meant to prevent cervical cancer, ended on Wednesday, with 2.281 million girls aged 9-14 vaccinated against a target of 2.843 million in the province, according to data released by the health department. A total of 4,040 teams, comprising 1,170 fixed, 2,553 outreach and 317 mobile ones, were deployed in the settled districts. Of the targeted girls, 620,978 remained unvaccinated. A total of 59.1pc girls were cases of refusal, 38.7pc were unavailable at home, while 2.2pc were ineligible for vaccination due to illness. Officials said the first phase of the country’s maiden HPV vaccination campaign was conducted in September last year in Punjab, Sindh, Islamabad and Azad Jammu and Kashmir. They said 9.6 million girls were vaccinated but none of the provinces or regions achieved the 90pc target. The World Health Organisation had asked KP and Balochistan authorities to step up public awareness ahead of the second phase, focusing on addressing weaknesses reported during the first phase. Sindh recorded the highest coverage at 68.6pc, while AJK reported the lowest at 39.2pc. In 12 designated districts of Khyber Pakhtunkhwa, 501,182 girls were vaccinated against a target of 743,745, achieving 67.4pc coverage. The remaining districts collectively achieved 84.8pc coverage, with 1,780,421 girls receiving the vaccine. According to the data, 833,741 girls were vaccinated in schools, while 1,451,064 received the vaccine through community-based vaccination activities. District-level performance reflected encouraging progress across the province. Upper Chitral recorded the highest coverage at 115pc , followed by Buner at 108pc, Lower Dir at 99pc, Lower Chitral at 98pc and Swat and Malakand at 96pc each. Among the designated districts, Bajaur achieved 87pc coverage, Mohmand 81pc and Lakki Marwat 80pc. The drive, conducted in collaboration with the education department, WHO, Unicef and Gavi, also marked the introduction of the 13th vaccine into the Expanded Programme on Immunisation. An official news release said professional medical associations, teachers, school administrations, lady health workers, frontline health workers, religious leaders, community representatives and media partners played an important role in the successful implementation of the campaign. Concerns regarding vaccine safety, infertility, parental consent and the purpose of vaccination were addressed through evidence-based information, counselling and dialogue. Safety monitoring mechanisms remained in place throughout the campaign, and no serious adverse event following immunisation was recorded. To ensure that girls who missed vaccination during the campaign are also protected, HPV vaccination services will continue at fixed vaccination centres from Nov 25 to 30. Director General (Health Services) Dr Naeem Awan expressed gratitude to his counterpart at the Federal Directorate of Immunisation, relevant departments and partner organisations for their support in making the campaign successful. Meanwhile, Dr Ashfaq Utmanzay, president of Malgari Doctran, has called for an increase in the remuneration of first-level supervisors, who served as medical officers in basic health units and performed duties from 9am to 4pm in remote areas during the campaign. He said the supervisors conducted rapid convenience assessments, maintained the cold chain, monitored team performance and resolved field issues on the spot but the compensation provided for travel, fuel, vehicle maintenance and food was less than their actual expenses. Time
The HPV vaccination campaign in Khyber Pakhtunkhwa (KP) was conducted over a two-week period, ending on Wednesday, November 24, 2026. The continuation of vaccination services from Nov 25 to 30, 2026, is mentioned in the evidence.
Location and population
The campaign took place in Khyber Pakhtunkhwa (KP), specifically in 12 designated districts and the remaining districts collectively. The target population was girls aged 9-14.
Event
Health workers in KP vaccinated more than 82% of the targeted girls during the HPV vaccination campaign, which aimed to prevent cervical cancer. The campaign was conducted in collaboration with the education department, WHO, Unicef, and Gavi.
Scale, impact and risk
A total of 2.281 million girls were vaccinated out of a target of 2.843 million. Of the targeted girls, 620,978 remained unvaccinated. The campaign achieved 84.8% coverage in the remaining districts and 67.4% in the 12 designated districts. The World Health Organisation had requested KP and Balochistan authorities to improve public awareness.
Response, status and uncertainty
The campaign was successfully implemented with the involvement of various stakeholders. Safety monitoring mechanisms were in place, and no serious adverse events were recorded. HPV vaccination services will continue at fixed centres from Nov 25 to 30, 2026, to cover those who missed the initial campaign.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Higher HPV viral loads tied to increased risk of cervical cancer - CIDRAP

News Brief
CIDRAP reported on a study linking higher human papillomavirus (HPV) viral loads to an increased risk of cervical cancer. The study highlights the potential correlation between the amount of HPV present in the body and the likelihood of developing cervical cancer. However, no specific case counts, death rates, or detailed prevention measures are provided in the evidence. The report serves as a research implication rather than a confirmed outbreak or event. The findings are part of a broader discussion on HPV-related diseases and the importance of understanding viral load impacts on health outcomes. This information underscores the need for further research and targeted public health strategies to address HPV-associated risks, particularly in cervical cancer prevention.

Cervicovaginal Microbiota Interventions Increase HPV Clearance and Cervical Lesion Regression - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报对 16 项临床试验的全面系统回顾和荟萃分析发现,恢复平衡的宫颈阴道微环境的干预措施可显着改善 HPV 清除和宫颈细胞异常的消退。这项发表在《微生物组前沿》上的研究显示,HPV 清除的汇总风险比为 1.39,宫颈异常消退的汇总风险比为 1.57,且未评估高级别病变或浸润性宫颈癌。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Cervicovaginal Microbiota Interventions Increase HPV Clearance and Cervical Lesion Regression - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A comprehensive systematic review and meta-analysis of 16 clinical trials found that interventions to restore a balanced cervicovaginal microenvironment significantly improved HPV clearance and regression of cervical cellular abnormalities. The study, published in Frontiers in Microbiomes, showed a pooled risk ratio of 1.39 for HPV clearance and 1.57 for regression of cervical abnormalities, with no high-grade lesions or invasive cervical carcinoma evaluated.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Saturday, November 28, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Biology Cervicovaginal Microbiota Interventions Increase HPV Clearance and Cervical Lesion Regression by November 27, 2026 in Biology Reading Time: 7 mins read 0 Cervicovaginal Microbiota Interventions Increase HPV Clearance and Cervical Lesion Regression Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram A comprehensive systematic review and meta-analysis published in Frontiers in Microbiomes reveals that clinical interventions designed to restore a balanced cervicovaginal microenvironment significantly accelerate human papillomavirus clearance and promote the regression of cervical cellular abnormalities. Persistent infection with high-risk human papillomavirus strains represents the established primary cause of more than ninety-five percent of all cervical cancers worldwide. Although approximately seventy-one percent of high-risk viral infections resolve spontaneously through natural host defenses, a critical subset establishes viral persistence that can drive malignant cellular transformation. However, only two to five percent of individuals with persistent infections eventually progress to high-grade squamous intraepithelial lesions or invasive carcinomas. This notable discrepancy demonstrates that viral exposure alone remains insufficient to trigger malignancy and confirms that secondary biological cofactors are required. A research team led by Larissa A. de Ridder from Radboud University Medical Centre systematically evaluated sixteen human intervention trials to determine whether modulating the cervicovaginal ecosystem enhances viral elimination and reverses early preneoplastic tissue changes. The cervicovaginal microenvironment plays a decisive role in modulating host susceptibility, local inflammation, and viral trajectory. In healthy individuals, the vaginal niche is typically dominated by beneficial Lactobacillus species, specifically within community state types classified as CST I, II, III, and V, which correspond to dominance by Lactobacillus crispatus, Lactobacillus gasseri, Lactobacillus iners, and Lactobacillus jensenii, respectively. These commensal bacteria convert host glycogen into lactic acid, maintaining an acidic biochemical barrier with a low pH that directly inhibits opportunistic pathobionts while manufacturing antimicrobial bacteriocins. Lactic acid additionally reinforces epithelial integrity by upregulating essential tight junction proteins and suppresses local inflammation through the downregulation of nuclear factor kappa-light chain enhancer of activated B cells, commonly known as NF-κB, which suppresses inflammatory cytokine production including interleukin-6 and tumour necrosis factor alpha. In contrast, dysbiotic states such as bacterial vaginosis, characterized by community state type IV, involve the depletion of protective lactobacilli and the rampant overgrowth of diverse anaerobic taxa such as Gardnerella, Fannyhessea, Prevotella, and Sneathia. This anaerobic state compromises epithelial integrity, induces mucosal inflammation, impairs antiviral defenses, and facilitates persistent viral oncogenesis. To investigate the clinical efficacy of therapies targeting this ecological interface, the investigators conducted a systematic search across PubMed, Embase, Web of Science, and the Cochrane Library for human trials published from January 2000 through September 2025. Registered prospectively in the PROSPERO database under identifier CRD420251104055 and executed according to PRISMA 2020 guidelines, the review screened 2,307 bibliographic citations before identifying sixteen eligible studies encompassing 2,490 female participants aged eighteen years and older with confirmed papillomavirus infections. The final evidence corpus comprised eight randomized controlled trials alongside eight non-randomized designs, including controlled clinical trials, prospective and retrospective cohort investigations, case-control analyses, and open single-arm pilot studies conducted across European and Asian medical centers. Across these protocols, eleven trials evaluated probiotic regimens utilizing defined live bacterial strains such as oral or vaginal Lactobacillus crispatus, Lactobacillus rhamnosus, Lactobacillus reuteri, and Lactobacillus plantarum, whereas five investigations evaluated non-probiotic microenvironment-modulating formulations, including carboxy-methyl beta-glucan gels, polycarbophil preparations, Coriolus versicolor extracts, and traditional herbal formulations like Jiawei Ermiao Granules. Discover more Engineering Job Board Research Paper Access Learn Engineering Track Biotech Tour Colleges Quantitative evidence synthesis demonstrated a statistically significant advantage for patients receiving microenvironment-targeted interventions over control cohorts. A random-effects Mantel-Haenszel meta-analysis pooling eleven controlled studies encompassing 1,588 participants established that targeted interventions significantly improved papillomavirus clearance, generating a pooled risk ratio of 1.39 with a 95% confidence interval ranging from 1.16 to 1.66. Substantial statistical heterogeneity was observed across these eleven datasets, reflected by an I-squared value of 63 percent, which the authors attributed to substantial variability in baseline patient populations, follow-up durations ranging from twelve weeks to fourteen months, and differing viral detection methodologies. Two included trials had examined probiotic interventions administered as adjunctive co-therapy alongside recombinant human interferon alpha-2b. To determine whether concurrent antiviral medication skewed the aggregate outcome, the researchers performed a planned sensitivity analysis excluding those two trials. The resulting pooled risk ratio remained stable and statistically significant at 1.40 with a 95% confidence interval of 1.11 to 1.77 and an I-squared value of 67 percent, confirming that the observed viral clearance effect was not driven by concomitant interferon pharmacotherapy. The meta-analysis revealed an even more uniform therapeutic effect regarding the regression of cervical cellular abnormalities. Pooling six controlled clinical studies comprising 1,152 participants that recorded standardized negative follow-up cytology demonstrated that microecological interventions increased the likelihood of cytological regression by fifty-seven percent compared with control interventions, yielding a risk ratio of 1.57 with a 95% confidence interval of 1.38 to 1.78. Remarkably, statistical heterogeneity across these six studies was entirely absent, characterized by an I-squared value of zero percent. Across the individual studies evaluated, baseline cytological presentations primarily encompassed atypical squamous cells of undetermined significance, known as ASCUS, and low-grade squamous intraepithelial lesions, known as LSIL, alongside histologically verified cervical intraepithelial neoplasia grade 1 lesions. Interventions consistently accelerated cytological normalization back to findings negative for intraepithelial lesion or malignancy, known as NILM. In specific trials, such as evaluations of the polycarbophil and beta-glucan gel Colpofix, therapeutic application achieved a 48.9 percent cytological normalization rate compared with 31.9 percent in untreated controls, accompanied by significant reductions in colposcopic anomalies and histological lesion persistence. Exploratory subgroup analyses were conducted across multiple clinical and pharmacological parameters to identify key drivers of treatment response and isolate sources of statistical heterogeneity. When stratified by administration route, vaginal interventions evaluated across six controlled studies comprising 976 participants yielded a statistically significant increase in viral clearance, producing a risk ratio of 1.49 with a 95% confidence interval of 1.21 to 1.84. In comparison, oral formulations evaluated across five studies involving 612 patients produced a more modest, non-significant risk ratio of 1.15 with a 95% confidence interval of 0.94 to 1.41. Although vaginal delivery achieved numerically superior viral eradication, the statistical test for subgroup differences did not achieve significance at a p-value of 0.08, and vaginal trials retained moderate internal heterogeneity with an I-squared of 61 percent. Conversely, when examining cytological regression, both administration pathways produced robust and comparable therapeutic gains: oral delivery generated a risk ratio of 1.42 with an I-squared of zero percent, while local vaginal administration generated a risk ratio of 1.61 with an I-squared of 6.8 percent, without significant divergence between routes. Subgroup analyses contrasting intervention categories further confirmed that therapeutic benefits were not restricted to living bacterial cultures. When classified by therapeutic mechanism, probiotic agents demonstrated a pooled risk ratio of 1.26 with a 95% confidence interval of 1.07 to 1.49 for viral clearance across seven controlled trials, while non-probiotic microenvironmental agents achieved a pooled risk ratio of 1.64 with a 95% confidence interval of 1.08 to 2.50 across four trials, with no statistically significant variance between classes at a p-value of 0.25. Regarding cytological regression, probiotic formulations yielded a risk ratio of 1.58, mirroring the 1.56 risk ratio achieved by non-probiotic agents. Among specific microbial strains, preparations containing Lactobacillus crispatus, such as the oral Crispact formulation containing strain M247, demonstrated a pooled clearance risk ratio of 1.50 with an I-squared of zero percent, whereas other probiotic preparations demonstrated a pooled risk ratio of 1.22. However, the comparative difference between bacterial strains did not achieve statistical significance at a p-value of 0.33, preventing the authors from declaring strain superiority. Ten of the included trials integrated microbiological and molecular profiling to assess whether observed clinical recoveries were accompanied by objective restructuring of the cervicovaginal microecology. Conventional microbiological assessments across these investigations documented marked therapeutic improvements, characterized by significant reductions in standardized Nugent scores, normalization of vaginal pH toward healthy acidic thresholds at or below 4.5, and decreased enzymatic activities of sialidase, catalase, and leukocyte esterase, which serve as classical biochemical surrogates of bacterial vaginosis and local mucosal inflammation. Advanced sequencing technologies, including 16S ribosomal RNA gene sequencing, internal transcribed spacer sequencing, and shotgun metagenomic profiling, confirmed pronounced taxonomic restructuring. Interventions stimulated dramatic expansions in relative Lactobacillus abundance while suppressing pathogenic anaerobic genera such as Gardnerella, Prevotella, and Sneathia. Multiple trials documented distinct ecological conversions wherein dysbiotic community state type IV profiles converted into protective, Lactobacillus crispatus-dominated community state type I structures, which were accompanied by significant decreases in microbial alpha diversity and prominent compositional shifts across multivariate beta diversity analyses. Despite these encouraging quantitative outcomes, the authors emphasized critical methodological limitations that necessitate cautious clinical interpretation. Risk of bias assessments conducted using the Cochrane RoB 2 framework judged all eight randomized controlled trials to possess some concerns, predominantly arising from inadequate documentation of randomization sequence generation, insufficient blinding protocols, or potential selective outcome reporting. Non-randomized studies evaluated with the ROBINS-I instrument exhibited moderate to critical risks of bias linked to unadjusted confounding factors, non-consecutive participant selection, and absent concurrent control cohorts. Applying the Grading of Recommendations Assessment, Development and Evaluation system, the authors rated the overall certainty of evidence as low for human papillomavirus clearance due to serious risk of bias and unexplained statistical inconsistency, and moderate for the regression of cervical abnormalities due to risk of bias limitations. Furthermore, substantial clinical heterogeneity in baseline diagnostic definitions, variable follow-up schedules, and diverse diagnostic criteria prevented the mathematical application of reliable funnel plot analyses to formally evaluate potential publication bias. The study authors underlined that all reviewed clinical trials relied exclusively on surrogate primary endpoints—specifically viral genome clearance and early cytological normalization—rather than definitive oncology outcomes. None of the sixteen trials evaluated high-grade squamous intraepithelial lesions, histologically confirmed cervical intraepithelial neoplasia grade 2 or higher, or the primary prevention of invasive cervical carcinoma. In addition, the vast majority of studies enrolled women presenting with pre-existing low-grade lesions where spontaneous physiological regression occurs naturally at high rates, and relied upon standard viral DNA amplification assays that cannot distinguish between self-limiting transient surface contamination and transcriptionally active viral integration. The researchers also noted that baseline dysbiosis was rarely confirmed objectively prior to study enrollment, meaning observed benefits reflect general microenvironmental modulation rather than the isolated correction of documented bacterial vaginosis. Consequently, the research team concluded that while current evidence demonstrates a clear, statistically robust association between cervicovaginal microenvironment optimization, accelerated viral clearance, and early lesion regression, these findings remain hypothesis-generating until validated by rigorous, adequately powered randomized controlled trials measuring standardized histological endpoints. Subject of Research: Microbiome-targeted interventions for cervicovaginal dysbiosis, HPV clearance, and cervical abnormality regression Article Title: Impact of correcting vaginal dysbiosis on the course of hrHPV infection and abnormal cytologies: a systematic review and meta-analysis Article References: de Ridder, L. 地点与对象

Vaginal Microbiome Imbalance Associates With Cervical Dysplasia and HPV in Togolese Women - Bioengineer.org

News Brief

Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Saturday, November 28, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Vaginal Microbiome Imbalance Associates With Cervical Dysplasia and HPV in Togolese Women by November 26, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 6 mins read 0 Vaginal Microbiome Imbalance Associates With Cervical Dysplasia and HPV in Togolese Women Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram In a community-based cervical cancer screening study conducted in Lomé, Togo, researchers identified significant associations linking vaginal microbial dysbiosis with high-risk human papillomavirus infection and cervical dysplasia among asymptomatic adult women. Evaluating a screening cohort of 338 women, investigators observed an overall high-risk human papillomavirus prevalence of 31.33 percent and detected cytological cervical epithelial abnormalities in 10.06 percent of participants. Women presenting with high-risk viral infections exhibited significantly elevated Nugent scores compared to uninfected participants, reflecting a marked phenotypic disturbance in baseline vaginal bacterial composition. A parallel elevation in Nugent scores was documented among individuals diagnosed with cervical dysplasia compared to those showing normal cytology. Rather than identifying a single infectious etiology, subsequent molecular analyses demonstrated that these clinical outcomes were characterized by a structural depletion of protective Lactobacillus species and a concurrent expansion of diverse polymicrobial anaerobic consortia, providing novel insights into mucosal cofactors within sub-Saharan Africa. Cervical cancer represents a severe public health crisis in sub-Saharan Africa, where limited screening infrastructure and constrained access to clinical interventions drive elevated morbidity and mortality rates. In Togo, cervical malignancy is the second most frequent cancer among female populations, responsible for an estimated age-standardized incidence of roughly 21.5 cases per 100,000 women and generating more than 400 annual deaths. While persistent infection with high-risk human papillomavirus genotypes is widely established as the primary causal driver of cervical intraepithelial neoplasia and invasive carcinoma, viral presence alone does not invariably lead to malignant progression. The vast majority of acquired viral infections are successfully cleared by host immune mechanisms within one to two years without causing persistent epithelial damage. Emerging scientific evidence has increasingly highlighted the cervicovaginal microenvironment, particularly mucosal microbial equilibrium, as an influential biological cofactor modulating viral persistence, local tissue inflammation, and early neoplastic transformation. However, mucosal microbiome research has historically remained skewed toward cohorts in industrialized nations, leaving microbial profiles among West African women largely uncharacterized prior to this community investigation. To investigate cervicovaginal microbial features associated with viral carriage and cervical abnormalities, investigators recruited sexually active, non-pregnant adult women aged 18 years or older through social media announcements and radio broadcasts in Lomé. Participants reported to the Association Togolaise pour le Bien-être Familial clinic for comprehensive cervical screening. Study eligibility required participants to be asymptomatic, in good general health, and without prior histories of cervical cancer, precancerous treatments, loop electrosurgical excision procedures, conization, or adnexectomy. To eliminate confounding biological exposures, the protocol excluded women who were actively menstruating, had engaged in sexual intercourse within the preceding three days, had received human papillomavirus vaccination, were currently receiving antibiotics or had taken them within three months, or presented known immune deficiencies. A total of 428 women completed structured face-to-face interviews documenting sociodemographic characteristics, reproductive histories, and lifestyle habits. Following these initial evaluations, 338 participants completed pelvic examinations, provided cervical samples for high-risk viral detection and Papanicolaou cytological assessment, and contributed vaginal swab specimens for standardized Nugent scoring. Discover more Track Fertility See an OB-GYN Find Cancer Care Find an OBGYN Study Anatomy Clinical evaluations integrated cytological assessment, phenotypic bacterial vaginosis grading, and molecular viral genotyping to characterize each participant. Conventional cervical cytology smears were stained according to the Papanicolaou protocol using automated instrumentation and independently reviewed by three pathologists using the Bethesda 2014 classification system. Smears were categorized as negative for intraepithelial lesion or malignancy, atypical squamous cells of undetermined significance, atypical squamous cells that cannot exclude high-grade lesions, low-grade squamous intraepithelial lesions, or high-grade squamous intraepithelial lesions. Concurrently, vaginal smears were Gram-stained to calculate Nugent scores from 0 to 10 based on relative morphotype counts of large Gram-positive rods, small Gram-negative rods, and curved bacilli. Genomic DNA was extracted from swab specimens using column-based purification kits. High-risk viral genotyping was executed utilizing the Anyplex II HPV HR Detection assay on a Bio-Rad CFX96 real-time PCR platform. This multiplex real-time polymerase chain reaction assay simultaneously amplified the L1 region across 14 high-risk viral genotypes alongside human beta-globin control genes, applying melting curve analysis across defined cycle thresholds to determine semi-quantitative viral load levels. Genotyping across the 338 screened participants revealed a diverse landscape of oncogenic viral types, led predominantly by HPV16, which accounted for 35.33 percent of all detected high-risk infections. The second most prevalent genotype was HPV51 at 14.66 percent, followed by HPV35 at 9.33 percent, HPV52 at 8.90 percent, HPV56 at 8.66 percent, and HPV58 at 7.33 percent. Genotypes HPV18 and HPV59 each represented 6.66 percent of infections, while HPV31, HPV66, HPV45, and HPV39 accounted for 3.66 percent, 2.66 percent, 1.94 percent, and 1.33 percent, respectively. Cytological examinations demonstrated that 10.06 percent of the screened cohort harbored cervical squamous abnormalities, including low-grade intraepithelial lesions, atypical squamous cells, and high-grade intraepithelial lesions, alongside pervasive evidence of local tissue inflammation. Women presenting cervical dysplasia were significantly older on average than those with negative cytology, whereas parity, age at sexual debut, and lifetime partner counts showed no statistically significant differences between groups. Multivariable logistic regression confirmed that high-risk viral carriage, early sexual debut, and higher parity served as independent predictive variables associated with elevated Nugent-defined vaginal dysbiosis. For deep molecular characterization of the mucosal ecosystem, a subset of 150 satisfactory vaginal DNA specimens fulfilling rigorous cellularity and purity thresholds underwent high-throughput 16S ribosomal RNA gene sequencing. Amplicons spanning the hypervariable V3-V4 region were generated using universal 319F and 806R primers, purified with magnetic beads, indexed through dual-indexing adapters, and sequenced on an Illumina MiSeq platform utilizing paired-end 300-base-pair reads. Raw sequence data were processed through the DADA2 pipeline in the R software environment for quality filtering, read denoising, paired-end merging, and chimera removal, yielding 5,110 unique amplicon sequence variants across 148 fully analyzed samples at an average read depth of 23,810 reads per library. Taxonomic classification was executed using the Ribosomal Database Project classifier matched against SILVA reference databases, supplemented by high-resolution secondary BLASTn alignments against the NCBI RefSeq 16S ribosomal RNA database requiring at least 99 percent sequence identity and 98 percent query coverage to assign putative species-level classifications conservatively. Rarefaction analysis demonstrated complete sequencing saturation, with Rarefaction Saturation Index values reaching 99.99 percent across all clinical subgroups, confirming that observed community metrics were not biased by uneven sequencing depth. Alpha diversity evaluations evaluating Chao1 richness, Shannon diversity, and Simpson evenness demonstrated no statistically significant differences between high-risk viral-positive and viral-negative women, nor between dysplastic and normal cytology groups. Specifically, mean Shannon diversity remained virtually indistinguishable between viral-positive women and uninfected controls at 3.68 and 3.67, respectively. Global beta diversity evaluated through Principal Coordinate Analysis of Bray-Curtis dissimilarity matrices revealed that viral infection status did not induce significant community restructuring across global coordinate planes. Similarly, Permutational Multivariate Analysis of Variance indicated that cervical dysplasia displayed only a modest, non-significant divergence trend, while community ecological volatility assessments remained statistically comparable across groups. These findings demonstrated that viral carriage and early cytological lesions do not cause complete wholesale ecological collapses, but instead correspond to selective compositional substitutions within an otherwise globally intact vaginal ecosystem. Despite stable global community metrics, fine-scale differential abundance analyses using negative binomial generalized linear models in DESeq2 revealed profound compositional rearrangements. Healthy, lesion-negative women exhibited vaginal microbiomes dominated by Firmicutes and protective Lactobacillus species. In contrast, participants carrying high-risk human papillomavirus or presenting cervical dysplasia demonstrated an extensive depletion of lactobacilli accompanied by substantial enrichment of opportunistic and strict anaerobic bacteria. Enriched taxa included Atopobium vaginae, Fannyhessea vaginae, Sneathia sanguinegens, Peptostreptococcus anaerobius, Peptoniphilus faecalis, and multiple Prevotella species. Stratified validation analyses restricted strictly to confirmed low-grade and high-grade squamous intraepithelial lesions while excluding indeterminate atypical cytological classifications confirmed that these anaerobic signatures remained significantly elevated, exhibiting log2 fold changes exceeding 2.5. Concurrently, protective Lactobacillus crispatus and Lactobacillus mulieris species remained significantly depleted in confirmed dysplasia cases. High-resolution heatmaps confirmed that dysplasia was linked to the coordinated expansion of polymicrobial anaerobic consortia rather than the isolated proliferation of an individual pathogen. Ecosystem classification into recognized human vaginal Community State Types demonstrated that CST IV, defined by high bacterial diversity and severe Lactobacillus depletion, represented the dominant ecological state across the studied population. CST IV was identified in 76.7 percent of healthy cytological controls and 81.5 percent of participants diagnosed with cervical dysplasia. CST III, characterized by an intermediate dominance of Lactobacillus iners, appeared in 15.0 percent of controls and 18.5 percent of dysplastic women, whereas classic Lactobacillus-dominated configurations designated as CST Other were found in only 8.3 percent of healthy controls and were completely absent among women with cervical dysplasia. Spearman correlation analysis indicated that quantitative human papillomavirus viral burden was not significantly correlated with the degree of community dysbiosis, yielding a correlation coefficient of -0.006. This absence of linear correlation suggests that viral replication intensity and mucosal bacterial disruption operate through partially decoupled pathways influenced by host immunity, mucosal barrier integrity, or preexisting anaerobic colonization rather than direct viral load kinetics. Functional pathway profiling inferred through the PICRUSt2 pipeline and MetaCyc database revealed that mucosal dysbiosis was accompanied by significant metabolic reprogramming toward anaerobic carbohydrate utilization and cellular stress responses. Dysplastic states and viral infection were characterized by significant enrichment in sucrose degradation II pathways alongside activation of chorismate biosynthesis II, heme biosynthesis, and lysine biosynthesis networks. These metabolic adaptations reflect bacterial mechanisms for sustaining cellular survival, promoting biofilm formation, and maintaining chronic local mucosal inflammation. The study authors emphasized that because their protocol employed a cross-sectional design, observed microbial shifts represent statistical associations rather than proven causal relationships. Furthermore, 16S rRNA gene sequencing provides predicted metagenomic capacity rather than direct enzymatic activity, and the down-selection of screened participants to 148 sequenced samples introduces potential selection limitations. Nonetheless, as the first West African community-based study combining blinded cytology, Nugent scoring, and next-generation sequencing, these findings establish critical baseline evidence supporting the development of microbiome-targeted screening and intervention strategies across high-burden settings. Treatment De-Escalation Fails to Match Standard Chemoradiotherapy in HPV-Associated Oropharyngeal Cancers - Bioengineer.org

Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Saturday, November 28, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Cancer Treatment De-Escalation Fails to Match Standard Chemoradiotherapy in HPV-Associated Oropharyngeal Cancers by November 27, 2026 in Cancer, Health Reading Time: 6 mins read 0 Treatment De-Escalation Fails to Match Standard Chemoradiotherapy in HPV-Associated Oropharyngeal Cancers Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Human papillomavirus-associated head and neck cancers, predominantly comprising oropharyngeal squamous cell carcinomas, represent an escalating global health burden characterized by distinct clinical, demographic, and biological features. According to global epidemiological data from GLOBOCON, head and neck malignancies accounted for approximately four percent of all cancer diagnoses in 2022, representing 745,000 new cases worldwide. Over recent decades, the global incidence of viral-driven head and neck squamous cell carcinomas has increased by 36.5 percent, with viral etiology contributing to approximately 25 percent of all head and neck malignancies. This association demonstrates striking anatomical variation, underlying an estimated 30.8 percent of oropharyngeal cancers alongside 2.4 percent of laryngeal and 2.2 percent of oral cavity tumors. The epidemiological profile has shifted toward younger cohorts, typically presenting in patients aged 45 to 64 years who lack significant exposure to tobacco or alcohol. Men face a disproportionately higher burden, exhibiting a two- to four-fold increased risk compared to women, while high-risk viral types 16 and 18 account for 84.9 percent of viral-positive tumors. The viral oncogenic cascade begins when infectious particles penetrate basal epithelial stem cells through microlesions in squamous mucosal tissues, potentially facilitated by host surface receptors including integrin alpha-6, laminin-332, and heparan sulfate proteoglycans. Following internalisation, the viral life cycle remains closely coordinated with epithelial cell differentiation. Initial infection stages rely on early proteins E1 and E2 to support episomal DNA replication and genomic maintenance in proliferating basal layers. As infected keratinocytes differentiate, early viral proteins E5, E6, and E7 sustain proliferative capacity and block apoptotic pathways, while E4 mediates G2 cell-cycle arrest to facilitate viral genome amplification. Complete virion assembly occurs in the upper epithelial strata through expression of late capsid proteins L1 and L2. Chronic infection promotes host genome integration requiring double-strand DNA breaks, inducing severe genomic instability. High-risk E6 activates telomerase reverse transcriptase transcription through complex interactions with E6-associated protein, c-Myc, and NFX1, thereby counteracting telomere shortening and conferring cellular immortality. Discover more Tour Colleges Study Biology Study Chemistry Viral oncoproteins E6 and E7 cooperate intimately to disable host tumor suppression and override cell-cycle checkpoints. The E7 oncoprotein binds hypo-phosphorylated retinoblastoma protein, triggering its proteasomal degradation and liberating E2F transcription factors to drive continuous progression past the G1/S transition. Although E7 expression generates oncogenic stress signals that naturally activate cellular defenses such as p53, p16, and p21, concurrent expression of E6 thwarts apoptosis by targeting p53 for degradation via the ubiquitin-proteasome pathway mediated by E6-associated protein. In oropharyngeal squamous cell carcinoma, this biology produces pronounced clinical divergence from cervical cancer and viral-negative malignancies. Oropharyngeal tumors elicit vigorous anti-tumor immune responses featuring more than double the infiltration of virus-specific T cells and reduced regulatory T-cell activity compared to cervical counterparts. However, viral interference with pattern recognition receptors, particularly Toll-like receptors, alongside dysregulated DNA damage repair, hypoxia, and extracellular matrix remodeling, can generate protective microenvironmental niches that foster treatment resistance. Discover more Engineering Job Board Research Paper Access Learn Engineering Track Biotech Current international clinical guidelines from the National Comprehensive Cancer Network and European consensus bodies establish distinct staging and management protocols for p16-positive oropharyngeal squamous cell carcinoma. For patients presenting with early-stage disease, consensus recommendations endorse single-modality treatment utilizing either definitive intensity-modulated radiotherapy or transoral surgery with neck dissection. In locoregionally advanced settings, concurrent cisplatin-based chemoradiotherapy delivering 70 to 72 Gray of intensity-modulated radiation therapy remains the standard of care to achieve optimal locoregional control and overall survival. Following surgical resection, adjuvant radiotherapy or chemoradiotherapy is indicated upon identification of high-risk adverse features, including positive surgical margins or extranodal tumor extension. Despite superior overall survival and radiosensitivity compared to viral-negative malignancies, international guidelines explicitly caution against substituting cetuximab for cisplatin outside clinical trials. Furthermore, routine treatment de-escalation remains strictly investigational, as unvalidated modifications to established curative regimens risk compromising disease control in patients who otherwise carry favorable oncological prognoses. Multiple prospective randomized clinical trials have evaluated treatment de-escalation strategies intended to diminish long-term therapeutic toxicities, yet several pivotal investigations failed to demonstrate oncological safety. In the randomized phase III RTOG 1016 and De-ESCALaTE HPV trials, substituting cetuximab for cisplatin during radiation therapy produced significantly worse cancer control without reducing overall treatment toxicities. RTOG 1016 recorded a 5-year locoregional failure rate of 17.3 percent with cetuximab compared with 9.9 percent for cisplatin, while De-ESCALaTE documented a 2-year recurrence rate of 16.1 percent versus 6.0 percent. Similarly, the randomized phase II/III NRG-HN005 trial examined reduced-dose radiotherapy of 60 Gray combined with either cisplatin or nivolumab against standard 70 Gray chemoradiotherapy in non-smoking patients with p16-positive oropharyngeal tumors. At 2.2 years of median follow-up, estimated 2-year progression-free survival was 98.1 percent with standard therapy, compared to 88.6 percent with reduced radiation plus cisplatin and 90.3 percent with reduced radiation plus nivolumab, failing non-inferiority criteria. Surgical de-escalation approaches have also confronted substantial safety and functional hurdles in prospective clinical evaluations. The randomized ORATOR2 trial, which compared primary radiotherapy against transoral surgery with neck dissection and pathology-directed adjuvant therapy for early-stage disease, was terminated prematurely following two treatment-related deaths in the surgical arm. Among 61 randomized participants, two-year overall survival reached 100 percent in the radiotherapy cohort compared to 90 percent following surgery, while progression-free survival was 100 percent versus 86 percent, respectively. Concurrently, a cohort analysis associated with the prospective phase III PATHOS trial evaluated early functional recovery in 508 patients undergoing transoral robotic surgery or transoral laser microsurgery. Patients treated with robotic approaches experienced significantly higher nasogastric tube dependency rates at 45.0 percent compared to 7.9 percent for laser microsurgery, alongside modestly inferior swallowing scores. Although transoral robotic surgery combined with selective neck dissection provides effective tumor clearance, its functional advantages diminish substantially whenever adjuvant therapy is required. In recurrent or metastatic head and neck squamous cell carcinoma, systemic therapy represents the primary therapeutic option, traditionally relying on platinum-based chemotherapy incorporating gemcitabine and cisplatin. Immunotherapy targeting the PD-1/PD-L1 axis has established a crucial role following disease progression on platinum agents. The CheckMate 141 clinical trial validated nivolumab following platinum failure, while KEYNOTE-048 established pembrolizumab as preferred first-line therapy, either as monotherapy for tumors expressing PD-L1 with a combined positive score of at least one or combined with platinum and 5-fluorouracil. Recently, KEYNOTE-689 expanded pembrolizumab approval into the perioperative setting for resectable locally advanced disease. Although the tumor microenvironment of viral-positive malignancies displays heightened immune infiltration, viral status itself did not independently predict therapeutic benefit in pivotal trials, and overall response rates to monotherapy remain between 13 and 19 percent. Survival gains were driven primarily by PD-L1 expression rather than viral etiology, confirming durable benefits across both viral-positive and viral-negative patient cohorts. To overcome limited response rates and treatment resistance in recurrent or metastatic settings, investigators are developing innovative targeted therapeutics and combination immunotherapies. Therapeutic vaccines, including ISA-101, MEDI0457, and PDS0101, specifically target viral E6 and E7 oncoproteins to elicit robust antigen-specific cytotoxic T-lymphocyte responses. In a phase II clinical trial combining the synthetic peptide vaccine ISA101 with nivolumab in patients with HPV16-positive cancers, researchers observed an objective response rate of 33 percent and a median overall survival of 17.5 months. Additional early-phase evaluations, such as the VERSATILE-002 and CUE-101 clinical trials, are investigating adoptive T-cell transfer and engineered T-cell receptor platforms. Single-cell analyses have further uncovered distinct immune subsets, including tissue-resident natural killer cells designated trNK-1, which correlate with favorable prognosis and enhanced immunotherapy response. Concurrently, molecular inhibitors directed against downstream pathways such as PI3K/Akt and mTOR are being explored, with preclinical evidence demonstrating that dual PI3K and IGF1R inhibition or irreversible pan-HER blockade via afatinib can circumvent acquired therapeutic resistance. Beyond conventional pharmacology, emerging therapeutic platforms encompass oncolytic virotherapy, photodynamic therapy, gene-directed interventions, and nanomedicine formulations. Oncolytic viruses, notably engineered agents like T-Vec and Reolysin, selectively infect and destroy malignant cells while stimulating systemic anti-tumor immunity, showing promise when combined with checkpoint blockade. Photodynamic therapy offers a minimally invasive alternative for early-stage mucosal lesions and precancerous tissue changes. Experimental gene therapies utilize RNA interference, small interfering RNAs, and short hairpin RNAs to selectively silence viral E6 and E7 expression, while gene editing strategies endeavor to restore endogenous tumor suppressor pathways. Furthermore, nanomedicine provides advanced drug delivery, radiosensitization, and immunomodulatory platforms. Preclinical investigations of tetravalent nanovaccines such as Qbeta-HPVag demonstrate robust anti-tumor activity by activating dendritic cell subsets including cDC1, cDC2, and DC3, thereby driving intratumoral T-cell infiltration. Additionally, magnetic nanoparticle hyperthermia utilizes alternating magnetic fields to generate localized thermal ablation, presenting targeted options for localized disease control. Despite extensive therapeutic innovation, formidable clinical challenges persist across diagnosis, molecular monitoring, and long-term disease management. Early detection remains constrained by reliance on invasive tissue biopsies, while minimally invasive approaches like salivary diagnostics show sensitivities of 72 to 77 percent and specificities of 90 to 94 percent. Liquid biopsy tracking circulating tumor HPV DNA through droplet digital PCR and next-generation sequencing enables detection of minimal residual disease three to six months prior to radiological progression, yet lacks clinical standardization. Diagnostic complexity is compounded by pathology misclassification, where expert reviews reclassify half of uncommon histological cases. Furthermore, public health surveys reveal that only 30.6 percent of individuals possess HPV awareness, with merely 29.2 percent recognizing its oropharyngeal cancer link. Finally, recurrent disease develops in 20 to 25 percent of patients, frequently marked by acquired mutations in TP53, KMT2D, and PIK3CA, necessitating standardized molecular profiling and rigorous prospective clinical trials. Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Saturday, November 28, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Scaling HPV Screening Alongside Vaccination Could Eliminate Cervical Cancer in Zambia by November 26, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 6 mins read 0 Scaling HPV Screening Alongside Vaccination Could Eliminate Cervical Cancer in Zambia Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram A nationwide transmission modeling study has demonstrated that scaling up community-based human papillomavirus DNA screening alongside adolescent vaccination could reduce cervical cancer incidence in Zambia by 97 percent over the next four decades, placing the World Health Organization elimination threshold within practical reach by 2060. Conducted by researchers from Columbia University and the Zambia Ministry of Health, the investigation utilized an individual-based transmission model calibrated to national demographic records, local sexual partnership dynamics, and empirical epidemiological data. The primary findings establish that while routine vaccination of pre-adolescent girls serves as a vital cornerstone for long-term control, immunization alone produces minimal epidemiological impact during the initial twenty years of implementation because existing disease burdens reside almost exclusively among adult cohorts who remain unimmunized. By contrast, pairing school-based vaccination programs with an expansive molecular screening campaign capable of administering approximately 400,000 tests annually accelerates clinical gains dramatically, averting an estimated 34,000 cervical cancer cases by 2040 and more than 183,000 cumulative cases by 2060. Zambia currently confronts one of the highest cervical cancer burdens documented worldwide, characterized in 2020 by an estimated crude annual incidence of 34.1 cases per 100,000 women and an associated mortality rate of 20.5 per 100,000 women, corresponding to an observed case fatality ratio of roughly 60 percent. This public health crisis is intensified by exceptionally high baseline exposure to high-risk viral strains, with a 2022 national population survey indicating that approximately 30 percent of women aged 25 to 49 test positive for oncogenic human papillomavirus. The transmission dynamic is further compounded by the concurrent human immunodeficiency virus epidemic, where an estimated 14.2 percent of Zambian women aged 15 to 49 live with HIV. Zambia introduced visual inspection with acetic acid within HIV clinics in 2006 and initiated a national single-dose vaccination campaign for girls aged 9 to 14 in 2019, yet structural access barriers across rural territories have continually constrained diagnostic coverage. Under this collaborative framework designed by ICAP at Columbia University and the Zambia Ministry of Health, rural community health volunteers facilitate patient self-collection of vaginal specimens, which undergo rapid automated testing using portable battery-powered molecular equipment capable of delivering high-performance DNA results equivalent to provider-collected samples. Expanding this molecular see-and-treat protocol beyond HIV-dedicated settings to all women aged 25 to 49 establishes a practical programmatic mechanism to bypass facility deficits and extend effective secondary prevention across remote populations. Discover more Find Cancer Care See an OB-GYN cancer Cancer To rigorously evaluate the population-level consequences of this expanded diagnostic delivery model, the investigators constructed an individual-based computational model that simulated transmission mechanics and disease natural history across 50,000 synthetic individuals initialized from Zambia’s 2019 age and sex distribution. Demographic projections incorporated empirical survival schedules, age-stratified birth rates, and historical census data compiled by the United States Census Bureau. The computational architecture simulated annual time steps wherein individuals entered the sexually active population upon reaching age 15. Annual partnership formation was governed by Poisson distributions reflecting age-specific mean partnership frequencies derived from sub-Saharan African behavioral datasets, with mixing constrained to opposite-sex partnerships within a five-year age difference to mirror documented national marital age gaps. The model accounted for changing human immunodeficiency virus background dynamics by holding adult HIV incidence fixed at 0.31 percent overall, reflecting calibrated 2021 national survey findings, while assigning risk ratios to represent accelerated infection and progression rates among immunocompromised women receiving modern antiretroviral therapy. The biological natural history within the model tracked individual infection longevity across discrete disease compartments: uninfected susceptibility, active oncogenic infection, aggregated precancerous cervical lesion, invasive cancer, and cause-specific mortality. Drawing on established biological trajectories, the authors specified that 85 percent of newly acquired infections clear spontaneously within four years without inducing permanent tissue dysplasia. For individuals experiencing persistent infection, transition probabilities governed progression into an aggregated lesion state, where lesions were required to persist for a mandatory dwell time of at least five years before becoming capable of advancing to invasive malignancy. Model calibration aligned parameters against observed empirical benchmarks, identifying a calibrated cumulative progression probability of 0.30 from persistent lesion to cancer, an annual infection-to-lesion transition rate of 0.012, a median progression interval from viral acquisition to invasive disease of 27 years, and a median age at cancer presentation of 46 years, matching observed clinical profiles in southern Africa. Discover more Check Symptoms Book a Checkup Book Lab Tests Using these calibrated baseline parameters, the research team projected Zambian disease trajectories through 2064 across five standardized counterfactual scenarios to contrast isolated interventions against integrated programmatic scale-up. The reference counterfactual, designated as No Interventions, modeled the complete withdrawal of national testing and vaccination activities after 2024, causing projected cervical cancer incidence to climb from 34 cases per 100,000 women in 2020 to 49 cases per 100,000 by 2060 as population aging expanded the demographic pool of mature adult women. The second scenario, Vaccination Only, sustained a constant 73 percent single-dose coverage rate among girls aged 9 to 14 with an assumed lifelong vaccine efficacy of 90 percent, but discontinued secondary screening. The third scenario preserved ongoing visual inspection with acetic acid starting at 154,280 annual tests and expanding by 5 percent annually while maintaining historical prioritization for women living with HIV. The fourth and fifth scenarios modeled universal molecular screening for all women aged 25 to 49, initiating testing volumes at 200,000 and 400,000 molecular tests per year respectively, both incorporating annual 5 percent expansion. Analysis of the simulation trajectories showed that relying exclusively on adolescent immunization leaves national cancer rates largely unaffected for multiple decades. Under the Vaccination Only pathway, cervical cancer incidence declined by an imperceptible 3.4 percent over the initial twenty years through 2040, because vaccinated adolescents required decades to age into the peak demographic windows of oncogenic vulnerability. Although isolated immunization eventually yielded a 29 percent reduction in annual cancer incidence by 2050 and a 47 percent reduction by 2060, it averted only 1,000 cumulative cancer cases during the first twenty years and 57,000 cumulative cases across the full forty-year horizon. Continuing traditional visual inspection with acetic acid produced intermediate outcomes, achieving a modest 14 percent incidence reduction by 2040 and a 61 percent reduction by 2060, while averting 80,000 cumulative malignancies over forty years. The visual approach was constrained by lower operator-dependent diagnostic sensitivity, documented between 22.8 percent and 48 percent in Zambian clinical evaluations, and structural reliance on clinical examination rooms. Universal population screening utilizing molecular DNA assays yielded dramatic, immediate reductions in disease incidence, fundamentally outperforming visual triage. Expanding molecular testing capacity to 200,000 tests annually achieved a 40 percent decline in cervical cancer incidence within twenty years and an 88 percent reduction after forty years, averting more than 143,000 cumulative cases by 2060. Increasing molecular throughput to 400,000 annual tests reduced national incidence by 64 percent by 2040 and 97 percent by 2060, bringing crude incidence down to approximately 2 cases per 100,000 women. Furthermore, the 400,000 annual test scenario satisfied the global target of maintaining 70 percent of adult women up to date with screening guidelines by 2032, averting 34,000 incident malignancies by 2040, 94,000 by 2050, and 183,000 cumulative cases by 2060, while preventing over 18,000 premature deaths. In secondary exploratory analyses simulating eighty-year transmission dynamics across varying vaccination coverage levels without concurrent screening, the researchers examined the indirect herd protection conferred by extending human papillomavirus vaccination to school-aged boys. At low to intermediate coverage levels, adding boys to national immunization registers produced substantial secondary protection for women. Specifically, achieving 37 percent coverage among both sexes prevented more than 120,000 additional cervical cancer cases over eighty years compared to immunizing 37 percent of eligible females alone, demonstrating how male immunization suppresses circulating viral transmission throughout shared sexual networks. However, the marginal epidemiological benefit of male vaccination diminished sharply as female immunization rates reached higher thresholds; under a hypothetical 91 percent female coverage scenario, expanding coverage to boys yielded only 4,387 additional averted cases across eighty years. These findings indicate that while universal sex-neutral immunization offers an effective mechanism to bolster herd immunity when female uptake is constrained by social hesitancy or delivery logistics, prioritizing universal coverage among girls remains the most efficient allocation of constrained resources. The authors highlighted several critical methodological assumptions and real-world operational challenges that contextualize their projections. The mathematical architecture utilized an aggregated lesion compartment rather than differentiating specific histological grades of cervical intraepithelial neoplasia, and partnership formation relied on annual stochastic resampling rather than dynamic long-term partnership tracking. Moreover, while baseline projections incorporated an optimistic 73 percent female vaccination coverage rate, actual Zambian adolescent uptake dropped from 99 percent in 2019 to 44 percent in 2021 before recovering to an estimated 52 percent by 2025. HPV-Positive Head And Neck Cancers Show Higher PD-L1 Levels In Cross-Sectional Study - Bioengineer.org

Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Saturday, November 28, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health HPV-Positive Head And Neck Cancers Show Higher PD-L1 Levels In Cross-Sectional Study by November 24, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 6 mins read 0 HPV-Positive Head And Neck Cancers Show Higher PD-L1 Levels In Cross-Sectional Study Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram In an ambispective cross-sectional evaluation of 100 patients with histopathologically confirmed head and neck squamous cell carcinoma, tumors testing positive for human papillomavirus DNA demonstrated a significantly higher frequency of programmed death ligand-1 expression compared to HPV DNA-negative cases. The study, conducted at Government Medical College in Kannauj, Uttar Pradesh, India, identified PD-L1 positivity in 73.8 percent of HPV DNA-positive tumors compared with 53.4 percent of HPV DNA-negative specimens. This observation corresponded to an odds ratio of 2.45, indicating that the odds of expressing PD-L1 were more than twice as high among HPV DNA-positive malignant tissues. However, the investigators noted that because of the cross-sectional investigation framework, these findings represent an exploratory association rather than proof of biological causality, clinical prognosis, or direct therapeutic responsiveness. At the same time, overall PD-L1 expression across the entire unstratified patient cohort reached 62 percent, aligning broadly with previously reported literature rates. Head and neck squamous cell carcinoma represents a substantial global healthcare burden characterized by pronounced morbidity and mortality. The disease encompasses malignancies originating within the mucosal epithelium of the oral cavity, oropharynx, hypopharynx, and larynx, with squamous cell carcinoma accounting for more than 90 percent of all diagnosed cases. Despite continuous advancements in surgical interventions, radiotherapy schedules, and cytotoxic chemotherapy regimens, the overall five-year survival rate for affected individuals remains approximately 50 percent, largely driven by late clinical presentation, aggressive local recurrence, and distant metastatic dissemination. Historically, regular tobacco consumption and persistent alcohol use have served as the primary epidemiological risk factors driving neoplastic transformation. Over recent decades, however, persistent oncogenic infection with human papillomavirus, particularly high-risk genotype HPV-16, has emerged as an increasingly prominent etiological contributor, especially within the oropharynx. HPV-positive head and neck malignancies form a clinically distinct pathological subset characterized by improved therapeutic responsiveness and superior overall survival rates. Within cancer research, the interaction between tumor cells and the surrounding immune microenvironment has become a central focus for understanding immune evasion mechanisms. The biological axis formed by programmed death-1 and programmed death ligand-1 operates as an essential immune checkpoint pathway, wherein PD-L1 displayed on neoplastic cells and tumor-infiltrating immune cells binds to PD-1 receptors present on cytotoxic T lymphocytes. This binding event inhibits downstream T-cell activation, blunting antitumor immune responses and permitting neoplastic cells to escape surveillance. Aberrant overexpression of PD-L1 occurs across numerous human malignancies, including head and neck carcinomas, where it correlates with local progression and localized immune suppression. The clinical introduction of checkpoint inhibitors targeting this pathway, such as the monoclonal antibodies pembrolizumab and nivolumab, has provided demonstrable therapeutic efficacy in recurrent and metastatic head and neck cancers. Nevertheless, the predictive reliability of PD-L1 assessment remains variable across clinical trials due to marked intratumoral heterogeneity and methodological differences. Discover more Find Cancer Care cancers Cancers cancer Check Symptoms The study evaluated a hospital-based cohort of 100 histopathologically confirmed primary head and neck squamous cell carcinoma cases collected over an 18-month observational timeline. The patient population ranged from 35 to 75 years of age, with a cohort mean age of 56 years and a standard deviation of 10 years, concentrating primarily in the fifth and sixth decades of life. Demographic assessment revealed a notable male predominance consisting of 72 male patients and 28 female patients, yielding a male-to-female ratio of 2.6 to 1. Tobacco usage was documented in 68 percent of participants, while 52 percent reported routine alcohol intake. Topographically, the oral cavity represented the most frequent primary tumor site, followed by the oropharynx, larynx, and other localized anatomical regions. Pathological TNM staging classified 38 cases as early-stage disease, comprising stages I and II, while 62 cases presented with advanced-stage disease, spanning stages III and IV. Histological differentiation revealed 24 well-differentiated tumors, 52 moderately differentiated tumors, and 24 poorly differentiated tumors. Conducted as an ambispective observational investigation, the study incorporated both archival formalin-fixed paraffin-embedded tissue blocks and prospectively accrued primary surgical specimens following formal Institutional Ethics Committee approval granted on August 12, 2024. Prospectively enrolled patients provided written informed consent prior to tissue acquisition, whereas retrospective cases were evaluated using anonymized archival records meeting rigorous preservation criteria. The authors applied strict eligibility boundaries: only primary tumor specimens were analyzed, explicitly excluding recurrent lesions, metastatic deposits, and cases with prior exposure to neoadjuvant chemotherapy or radiotherapy. Specimens demonstrating compromised tissue integrity, insufficient tumor volume, incomplete clinical records, or missing essential laboratory data were excluded from downstream analysis. All qualified tissue specimens were fixed using standard 10 percent neutral buffered formalin, routinely processed through histological cassettes, and embedded in paraffin blocks. Serial microtome sections cut at three to four micrometers were stained with hematoxylin and eosin to confirm diagnosis and grade. Laboratory evaluations of PD-L1 expression were outsourced to an external accredited diagnostic facility utilizing validated automated immunohistochemical protocols. Staining was conducted on serial formalin-fixed paraffin-embedded sections of three to four micrometers mounted on positively charged glass slides using the primary anti-PD-L1 antibody clone CAL10 manufactured by Biocare Medical. Deparaffinization, rehydration, and heat-induced epitope retrieval using an ethylenediaminetetraacetic acid buffer at pH 9.0 were executed on an automated Ventana BenchMark platform from Roche Diagnostics. Endogenous peroxidase activity was chemically blocked prior to primary antibody incubation, and visualization was achieved using a polymer-based horseradish peroxidase detection system paired with diaminobenzidine chromogen and hematoxylin counterstaining without signal amplification. Formalin-fixed human tonsil tissue served as an external positive control, while negative controls omitted the primary antibody. Scoring utilized the Combined Positive Score, calculated as the absolute number of PD-L1-staining tumor cells, lymphocytes, and macrophages divided by total viable tumor cells, multiplied by 100, defining positivity as a score of one or greater. Microscopic evaluation of PD-L1 expression across all 100 head and neck squamous cell carcinoma specimens demonstrated positive staining in 62 cases, representing an overall positivity rate of 62 percent, while 38 cases demonstrated an absence of PD-L1 expression. Stratification across four mutually exclusive Combined Positive Score intervals revealed that 38 specimens exhibited a score below one, 24 specimens fell into the low-expression bracket ranging from one to nine, 16 specimens occupied the intermediate category spanning scores from 10 to 19, and 22 specimens demonstrated high expression with scores equal to or exceeding 20. Staining patterns were characterized by convincing membranous positivity along neoplastic cell borders alongside cytoplasmic or membranous staining within stromal immune infiltrates. The observed 62 percent overall positivity rate falls directly within the broad 46 percent to 92 percent range previously documented in published head and neck cancer literature. Statistical correlation analyses demonstrated that PD-L1 positivity did not significantly associate with patient age, sex, tumor anatomical site, histological differentiation, clinical stage, or lymph node metastasis. Evaluation of human papillomavirus status commenced with automated DNA testing using the cobas HPV platform on formalin-fixed paraffin-embedded tumor sections. The cobas assay individually discriminates high-risk genotypes HPV-16 and HPV-18 while simultaneously screening for a pooled group of 12 other high-risk genotypes, including HPV-31, 33, 35, 39, 45, 51, 52, 56, 58, 59, 66, and 68. Across the cohort, 42 cases tested positive for HPV DNA, while 58 cases were negative. Among the 42 positive cases, genotype HPV-16 accounted for 34 cases, whereas the remaining eight cases harbored other pooled high-risk genotypes. Topographical distribution demonstrated a statistically significant association with anatomical tumor site, where the oropharynx exhibited the highest infection frequency at 71.4 percent, representing 20 positive cases out of 28 tumors. Furthermore, HPV DNA positivity correlated significantly with histological differentiation: poorly differentiated carcinomas demonstrated a 70.8 percent positivity rate, compared with 38.5 percent in moderately differentiated tumors and 20.8 percent in well-differentiated tumors, reflecting characteristic viral morphologic patterns. To verify transcriptional viral activity beyond mere viral DNA presence, researchers performed RNA in situ hybridization utilizing the RNAscope 2.5 HD Reagent Kit with probes directed against high-risk HPV E6/E7 messenger RNA transcripts. Positive expression was recognized by punctate brown hybridization signals within the nuclei or cytoplasm of viable tumor cells. Comparative concordance analysis between cobas DNA testing and RNA in situ hybridization demonstrated strong diagnostic agreement across 95 of the 100 examined cases, yielding an overall concordance rate of 95.0 percent. Among the 42 cobas DNA-positive tumors, 39 showed concordant positive E6/E7 mRNA signals, while three tumors lacked detectable transcripts. Conversely, among 58 DNA-negative tumors, 56 were concordantly negative, and two exhibited positive mRNA hybridization. Statistical evaluation yielded a Cohen’s kappa coefficient of 0.89, establishing robust concordance between automated DNA detection and transcriptionally active messenger RNA identification. The authors emphasized that reserving the term HPV-associated disease for transcriptionally active, mRNA-confirmed cases prevents clinical misclassification arising from transient non-transcriptionally active viral colonization. Cross-tabulation of biomarker expression revealed that 31 of 42 HPV DNA-positive tumors expressed PD-L1 compared with 31 of 58 HPV DNA-negative tumors, confirming a statistically significant correlation with an odds ratio of 2.45. This observation supports the hypothesis that HPV-positive head and neck tumors frequently foster an immune-active microenvironment characterized by adaptive PD-L1 upregulation. Nonetheless, the authors outlined critical limitations governing their investigation. The cross-sectional design restricts analysis to observational associations, precluding inferences regarding biological causality, long-term survival, or therapeutic responsiveness. Furthermore, immunohistochemical assessment utilized the CAL10 antibody clone rather than an officially validated companion diagnostic assay, meaning these measurements cannot establish direct eligibility for therapeutic checkpoint inhibition. Additionally, the presence of discordant findings between DNA and RNA platforms underscores the necessity of standardizing molecular definitions for HPV status. Consequently, the authors concluded that these exploratory findings warrant validation through larger multicentric investigations incorporating standardized diagnostic assays, uniform testing algorithms, and longitudinal clinical outcome monitoring. Location and population
Aberrant overexpression of PD-L1 occurs across numerous human malignancies, including head and neck carcinomas, where it correlates with local progression and localized immune suppression.
