时间:2026年11月28日 来源:Medical Oncology 4.7 编辑推荐: 人乳头瘤病毒(HPV)相关头颈癌(HNCs),尤其是口咽鳞状细胞癌(OPSCC),构成日益加重的全球健康负担,近几十年来发病率快速上升。此类肿瘤在生物学和临床上不同于HPV阴性HNCs,更常见于传统危险因素如烟草和酒精暴露有限的年轻个体。HPV驱动的致癌过程由 为了促进科学的传播,生物通采集了这篇文章 订阅BioHot点这里解锁原文链接推送! “畅读会员”点此登录查看原文链接 Review Article Open access 人乳头瘤病毒(HPV)相关头颈癌(HNCs),尤其是口咽鳞状细胞癌(OPSCC),构成日益加重的全球健康负担,近几十年来发病率快速上升。此类肿瘤在生物学和临床上不同于HPV阴性HNCs,更常见于传统危险因素如烟草和酒精暴露有限的年轻个体。HPV驱动的致癌过程由病毒癌蛋白E6和E7介导,其通过使p53和视网膜母细胞瘤(Rb)通路失活破坏基因组稳定性,促进不受控制的增殖、基因组不稳定和治疗耐药。当前HPV阳性HNCs的治疗策略包括手术、放疗和化疗,以顺铂为基础的放化疗(CRT)仍是局部晚期疾病的标准治疗。然而,HPV阳性患者治疗反应和总体预后改善,促使研究人员关注在不损害疗效的前提下降低毒性的治疗降阶梯策略。微创手术方法,如经口机器人手术(TORS),以及降低强度的放疗方案显示出有前景的功能学结局。免疫治疗是复发和转移性疾病的重要进展,靶向PD-1/PD-L1轴的免疫检查点抑制剂在选定患者群体中显示生存获益。新兴方法包括针对HPV癌蛋白的治疗性疫苗、过继性T细胞转移、靶向治疗、溶瘤病毒治疗,以及旨在沉默E6/E7表达或恢复抑癌功能的基因治疗。纳米医学进一步为药物递送、放射增敏和免疫调节提供新平台,提高治疗特异性和疗效。尽管取得显著进展,重大挑战仍然存在,包括早期诊断的限制、治疗耐药、生物标志物驱动的患者选择以及公众对HPV相关癌症风险认识不足。整合先进诊断方法,如循环肿瘤HPV DNA检测和下一代测序(NGS),可能改善风险分层和治疗监测。持续研究联合治疗、靶向方法和患者定制治疗算法对于改善结局仍至关重要。总之,HPV相关HNCs不断演变的治疗策略反映了向精准肿瘤学的转变,重点关注改善生存、降低毒性以及由分子肿瘤特征驱动的个体化干预。 引言:HNCs传统上与吸烟和饮酒相关,但HPV日益成为尤其口咽部恶性肿瘤的重要病因。HPV可经口交、深吻、自体接种和垂直传播;性行为变化和烟草使用下降可能促成HPV相关HNC发病率上升。疾病可在感染后长达40年出现,患者常较年轻、健康且缺乏典型危险因素,导致诊断延迟。HPV阳性HNSCC通常生存优于阴性,但高HPV整合等亚组预后较差。HNC约90%为鳞状细胞癌,2022年全球约74.5万新病例;近几十年HPV相关HNSCC全球发病率增加36.5%,约25% HNC归因于HPV,口咽癌30.8%、喉癌2.4%、口腔癌2.2%;多数与HPV 16/18相关,疫苗是最有效预防手段并应覆盖男性。OPSCC风险分层由NCCN和ESMO共识标准化。本文整合最新降阶梯试验(包括NRG-HN005、ORATOR2、PATHOS)、当前指南推荐、免疫检查点抑制剂更新指征和新兴方法,并评价证据水平与实施障碍。 HPV相关HNCs的流行病学:全球HPV每年导致63万新癌症病例,占4.5%;酒精约5%、烟草34%、二者合并36%的HNC。HPV相关HNSCC发病率上升,危险因素和人口学特征不同于HPV阴性者。HPV相关HNSCC主要发生于口咽,HPV在其他头颈部位因果作用较弱;欧洲新发和死亡数高于美国。HPV阴性HNC多见于老年、社会经济地位较低和口腔卫生差者;HPV阳性者常较年轻(45–64岁)、非吸烟、无明显饮酒史,多个性伴侣尤其口交史为危险因素;男性风险为女性2–4倍,且存在种族差异。分子上HPV阳性肿瘤表达E6/E7并使p53和pRb失活;HPV阴性HNSCC常见TP53和细胞周期调控改变,预后较差。HPV阳性OPSCC放化疗反应和预后优于阴性;HPV在宫颈鳞状细胞癌(CSCC)和OPSCC均关键,但OPSCC放射敏感性更高,口咽局部免疫反应更强,HPV特异性T细胞浸润更多、调节性T细胞(Treg)活性更低。 HPV在癌症发生中的病理机制:WHO将14型HPV列为致癌型。HPV为DNA病毒,嗜鳞状上皮,微损伤使致癌型HPV感染基底上皮干细胞,潜在受体包括整合素α-6、层粘连蛋白-332和硫酸类肝素蛋白聚糖。E1/E2支持病毒复制和附加体维持;E5、E6、E7促进细胞周期进展并抑制凋亡,E4与G2阻滞相关;上层上皮表达L1/L2并组装释放病毒。病毒整合可致突变和染色体异常。HPV通过调节细胞周期、干扰DNA修复、诱导氧化应激和改变端粒长度诱导基因组不稳定;E6促进人端粒酶逆转录酶(hTERT)转录并增加端粒酶活性,延长增殖并延迟衰老。E6结合p53并促其蛋白酶体降解;E7结合pRb使其失活,释放E2F,促进细胞周期基因表达,并可致染色体复制错误和纺锤体异常。E7作为致癌应激激活p53和p16INK4A反应,但E6通过E6相关蛋白(E6AP)介导的泛素-蛋白酶体途径降解p53以抵消保护;二者协同促进永生化与恶性转化。耐药机制包括E6抑制凋亡、E7促进增殖、细胞周期和DNA修复紊乱、干扰Toll样受体(TLRs)等模式识别受体(PRRs)逃避先天免疫、激活DNA损伤反应和修复(DDR)通路,以及缺氧、细胞外基质(ECM)重塑和基质细胞相互作用形成保护性微环境。 HPV相关HNCs的当前治疗策略:历史上HPV阳性和阴性HNSCC治疗相同。局部晚期标准为顺铂为基础CRT,使用70–72 Gy调强放疗(IMRT);单纯放疗用于早期或降阶梯方案。NCCN和EHNS-ESMO-ESTRO将p16阳性口咽癌视为独立实体,AJCC第8版有独立分期;早期推荐根治性放疗或经口手术加颈清扫,局部晚期仍以顺铂为基础CRT为标准;术后结外侵犯或切缘阳性需辅助放疗或放化疗;不推荐西妥昔单抗替代顺铂,临床试验外降阶梯为研究性;复发/转移一线首选依据PD-L1联合阳性评分(CPS)选择的帕博利珠单抗方案。降阶梯试验未证明肿瘤学安全性:RTOG 1016和De-ESCALaTE中西妥昔单抗替代顺铂结局较差且未降低毒性;NRG-HN005中标准CRT 2年无进展生存(PFS)98.1%,减量放疗加顺铂88.6%、加纳武利尤单抗90.3%,均未达非劣效;ORATOR2因手术组两例治疗相关死亡提前终止,2年总生存(OS)放疗100% vs 手术90%,PFS 100% vs 86%。PATHOS相关队列显示TORS后鼻胃管依赖高于经口激光显微手术(TLM)(45.0% vs 7.9%)。TORS在T1–T2且无需辅助治疗时功能获益较明显,需辅助治疗时优势大多消失;TORS加选择性颈清扫(SND)与广泛手术生存相当。IMRT需准确靶区勾画,PET-CT可改变分期和靶区覆盖但缺乏生存和HPV特异性证据;3D打印证据限于小型回顾性系列。复发/转移以铂类联合吉西他滨和顺铂为一线,但疗效有限、毒性高,促使研究新辅助、减量放疗和手术先行。免疫治疗中纳武利尤单抗和帕博利珠单抗靶向PD-1/PD-L1,已用于铂类失败后及一线PD-L1阳性(CPS≥1)或联合铂类和5-氟尿嘧啶;围手术期帕博利珠单抗用于可切除局部晚期HNSCC且CPS≥1。HPV状态不预测免疫治疗获益,单药缓解率约13–19%,获益主要由PD-L1表达决定。治疗性疫苗ISA-101、MEDI0457、PDS0101靶向E6/E7并联合PD-1阻断;过继T细胞转移和工程化TCR在早期研究。NGS可检测PIK3CA、TP53、NOTCH1和表观遗传改变;微滴数字PCR(ddPCR)和循环肿瘤DNA(ctDNA)液体活检用于无创监测。APG-157和组织驻留自然杀伤细胞(trNK)亚群trNK-1等为探索性方向。 新兴治疗策略:许多方法主要由临床前数据支持,细胞系和动物模型不能完全再现人类肿瘤复杂性,需适当期临床试验确认。循环肿瘤HPV DNA(ctHPV DNA)是最有前景的液体活检之一,ddPCR和NGS可检测根治后微小残留病(MRD),可早于影像3–6个月提示复发,但需前瞻多中心验证、标准化方法和截断值。降阶梯旨在减少副作用并保留疗效,策略包括省略化疗、降低或省略放疗,可保留咽喉功能、减少长期毒性、改善生活质量。免疫检查点抑制剂是重要方向,帕博利珠单抗和纳武利尤单抗阻断PD-1/PD-L1,在复发/转移中有效,研究单用或与放化疗联合。靶向治疗包括表皮生长因子受体(EGFR)抑制剂、mTOR和PI3K/Akt通路抑制剂;PI3K抑制剂耐药可被胰岛素样生长因子1受体(IGF1R)抑制剂克服;阿法替尼抑制多个HER受体。溶瘤病毒如T-Vec和Reolysin选择性破坏癌细胞并刺激免疫,在临床试验中与检查点抑制剂联合有潜力。光动力治疗(PDT)用于早期癌症和癌前病变。基因治疗用RNAi、siRNA或shRNA沉默E6/E7;免疫原性基因治疗引入HPV抗原基因;恢复抑癌功能的基因编辑处于实验阶段。纳米颗粒可靶向递送药物、检查点抑制剂和放射增敏剂,降低全身毒性;纳米疫苗如四价纳米疫苗和Qbeta-HPVag在临床前模型增强树突细胞和T细胞活化;ISA101联合纳武利尤单抗II期客观缓解率(ORR)33%、中位OS 17.5个月;磁性纳米颗粒热疗主要在临床前评估。 HPV相关HNCs管理中的挑战:临床评估仍主要依赖侵入性方法,可能延迟诊断;ctHPV DNA液体活检精确但费用高、缺乏标准化。罕见亚型分类需专家病理复核,回顾研究中半数病例复核后重新分类、16%存在重大差异。公众认识不足,调查仅30.6%了解HPV、29.2%将其与口咽癌联系。唾液HPV16 E6/E7检测对p16阳性肿瘤灵敏度72%–77%、特异度90%–94%,但受设备和人员限制。数字健康技术可远程监测症状、体重、营养和毒性,但缺乏HPV相关HNC证据,数据安全和成本待解决。约20–25%HPV阳性HNSCC复发,与肿瘤异质性、免疫逃逸和癌症干细胞样细胞有关;复发癌可见HPV基因活性和染色质结构改变。免疫治疗耐药与肿瘤差异和免疫逃逸有关,联合放疗或靶向治疗正在研究。降阶梯仍有争议:RTOG 1016中5年局部区域失败率西妥昔单抗17.3% vs 顺铂9.9%,De-ESCALaTE HPV中2年复发率16.1% vs 6.0%,故符合条件者仍以顺铂为基础CRT为标准。复发/转移HPV阳性HNSCC可获TP53突变(约15%)、KMT2D和PIK3CA突变,提示更侵袭表型和治疗反应降低。 结论:局部晚期HPV阳性HNSCC仍以顺铂为基础CRT为标准,早期和选定低风险患者探索降阶梯,TORS在选定患者有功能获益;复发/转移以全身治疗为主,免疫治疗、靶向治疗和联合策略因毒性担忧而发展;溶瘤病毒、基因治疗、基因编辑和纳米技术等需进一步临床评价。挑战包括无创诊断实施有限、公众认识不足、高成本、缺乏标准化检测、治疗耐药、肿瘤异质性和分子演变,以及免疫治疗患者选择困难。未来应验证可靠生物标志物以改善风险分层,前瞻多中心验证ctHPV DNA并标准化检测和监测间隔,优化降阶梯并识别可安全减量者,将分子谱和NGS整合临床决策。 打赏 展开全文 开通VIP 享海量文章 全站开放获取+解锁超过 200万篇新闻全文+每日更新 2000+篇,学术前沿动态全时追踪! 从2000到2026,27载全因有您;您的每一分支持,都给予我们无穷的动力! 公测期:打赏8元解锁30天免费阅读! Advertisement News Cancer HorizonsAll NewsBlogsCaregivingDiet and ExerciseFinancialPsychosocialRecipesSexual HealthSide EffectSponsoredSurvivorship Watch Expert ConversationsShows & PodcastsNews & Analysis Conferences Conference Coverage Conference Listing Events CURE AdventuresCURE AwardsEducated Patient In Person EducationEducated Patient Virtual Education More Resources Advocacy GroupsArt GalleryClinical Trial CornerHeal®PartnersPodcastsPublicationsShare Your Story Subscribe My Account News Watch Conferences Events Resources Subscribe Blood Cancers MPN MDS Myeloma Brain Cancer Breast Cancer Metastatic Breast Cancer Childhood Cancers Gastrointestinal Cancers Colorectal Cancer Esophageal Cancer Gastrointestinal stromal tumor Liver Cancer Pancreatic Cancer Genitourinary Cancers Bladder Cancer Kidney Cancer Prostate Cancer Gynecologic Cancer Cervical Cancer Ovarian Cancer Head & Neck Cancer Leukemia Acute Lymphoblastic Leukemia Acute Myeloid Leukemia CLL Lung Cancer Small Cell Lung Cancer (SCLC) Lymphoma Follicular Lymphoma Mantle Cell Lymphoma Non-Hodgkin Lymphoma Rare Cancers Neuroendocrine Cancer Sarcoma Skin Cancer Advanced Melanoma Thyroid Cancer Cancer Type Spotlight - Blogs| Breast Cancer Webinar Series| Cancer Horizons| Clinical Trial Corner| Heal®| Publications| Videos Advertisement News|Articles|November 26, 2026 Infections Cause 1 in 8 Cancers Worldwide, Global Study Finds Author(s)Spencer Feldman Fact checked by: CURE staff Listen 0:00 / 6:55 Key Takeaways Infections contributed ~1 in 8 new cancers globally, implying substantial preventable incidence using existing modalities including HPV/HBV vaccination, cervical screening, and antimicrobial/antiviral therapy. *H. pylori* and HPV were the largest contributors (~760k and ~750k cases), followed by HBV (~360k), EBV (~260k), and HCV (~160k), mapping to gastric, anogenital/head-neck, hepatic, and nasopharyngeal malignancies. Expanded attribution incorporated additional infection–cancer links, including HIV-associated malignancy risk via immunosuppression and coinfections, plus Merkel cell polyomavirus–related Merkel cell carcinoma. Geographic inequities were pronounced, with >75% of infection-attributable cancers in LMICs; Eastern Asia was dominated by *H. pylori*/HBV, while sub-Saharan Africa showed high HPV and HIV/KSHV-associated burdens. Prevention priorities include scale-up of vaccines and screening, test-and-treat programs for *H. pylori*, HBV/HCV, and HIV, and R&D investment for EBV, *H. pylori*, and KSHV vaccines. SHOW MORE Infections caused about 2.3 million new cancers worldwide in 2024, a global study found. Vaccines, screening and treatment could prevent many of them. Advertisement Infections caused about 2.3 million new cancers worldwide in 2024, a global study found. Vaccines, screening and treatment could prevent many of them. About 1 in 8 new cancers diagnosed worldwide in 2024 was caused by an infection, according to a global analysis from the International Agency for Research on Cancer (IARC), the World Health Organization's cancer research agency. The study, published Sept. 28 in The Lancet Oncology, estimated that infections caused about 2.3 million new cancer cases in 2024, or 12% of all new cases. Many of those cancers could be prevented with tools that already exist, including vaccines, screening and treatment for the infections themselves. "The findings underscore the major role that infection control can play in reducing the global cancer burden," Harriet Rumgay, a scientist in IARC's Cancer Surveillance Branch and an author of the study, said in a news release. Which Infections Cause the Most Cancers? Five infections accounted for almost all infection-related cancers, according to IARC: Helicobacter pylori (H. pylori), a stomach bacterium: about 760,000 cases, mainly stomach cancer Human papillomavirus (HPV): about 750,000 cases, mainly cervical cancer, along with anal, vulvar, vaginal, penile and some head and neck cancers Hepatitis B virus: about 360,000 cases, mostly liver cancer Epstein-Barr virus: about 260,000 cases, largely nasopharyngeal cancer (cancer of the upper throat behind the nose), plus some stomach cancers and Hodgkin lymphoma Hepatitis C virus: about 160,000 cases, mostly liver cancer The researchers studied 12 infections known to cause cancer. Their analysis added 16 infection-cancer links that earlier global estimates did not include, among them cancers associated with HIV and with Merkel cell polyomavirus, a virus linked to a rare skin cancer called Merkel cell carcinoma. Recommendations tailored to you Sign in or create an account to see articles, videos, and podcasts matched to your specialties. Sign in or sign up HIV does not cause cancer directly, the National Cancer Institute (NCI) explains, but it raises the risk of several cancers, including Kaposi sarcoma, lymphomas and cancers of the cervix, anus, lung, liver and throat. Is Cancer Contagious? No. "A person cannot catch cancer from someone else," according to the American Cancer Society (ACS). What can spread is the infection. Many of the infections that raise cancer risk can pass from person to person, and some can be passed through blood or other body fluids, according to NCI. Infections can lead to cancer in a few ways, the ACS notes. Some viruses directly affect the genes that control how cells grow, some cause long-term inflammation and some weaken the immune system's ability to find and destroy abnormal cells. Even so, most people with these infections never develop cancer. Where Are Infection-Related Cancers Most Common? More than three-quarters of infection-related cancers occurred in low- and middle-income countries, according to IARC. Eastern Asia alone accounted for 42% of infection-related cancers worldwide, with a particularly high burden from H. pylori and hepatitis B. Sub-Saharan Africa had very high rates of HPV-related cancers, plus a substantial burden from HIV and Kaposi sarcoma-associated herpesvirus. IARC said prevention tools are often underused or out of reach, particularly in low- and middle-income countries, because of financial, social and political barriers. How Can Infection-Related Cancers Be Prevented? IARC points to several approaches: Vaccination: Vaccines against HPV and hepatitis B already exist. IARC also called for investment in new vaccines against Epstein-Barr virus, H. pylori and Kaposi sarcoma-associated herpesvirus, for which effective prevention options are not currently available. Screening: Cervical cancer screening is one of the main prevention tools IARC highlighted. Testing and treatment: H. pylori, hepatitis B, hepatitis C and HIV can be tested for and treated. NCI notes that H. pylori infection can be detected and treated with antibiotics, and recommends seeing a doctor about stomach problems. What Should Patients and Families Ask Their Doctors? If you have questions about HPV vaccination for yourself or your children, testing for hepatitis or H. pylori, or cervical cancer screening, your primary care provider is a good place to start. For people already in cancer treatment, hepatitis B can matter in another way. CURE explains why hepatitis B matters during cancer treatment and who needs testing. References Rumgay H, Georges D, Huang Y, et al. Global burden of cancer attributable to infections in 2024: a worldwide incidence analysis. The Lancet Oncology. 前瞻性研究中 HPV 病毒载量轨迹预测 宫颈癌 风险 - Bioengineer.org

HPV Viral Load Trajectories Predict Cervical Cancer Risk in Prospective Study - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报中国常州的一项前瞻性研究发现,HPV 病毒载量随时间变化的轨迹可以预测 宫颈癌 风险。病毒载量持续较高的女性发生宫颈癌前病变的风险为 90.4%,而低稳定组的风险为 10.9%。这项研究通过纳入纵向病毒趋势,提供个性化的后续方案,改善了临床风险分层。

时间

虽然高危 人乳头瘤病毒 菌株的持续感染长期以来一直被认为是宫颈恶性肿瘤 发病机制 的核心,但病毒浓度的纵向变化在临床研究中基本上仍未得到检验。
事件

HPV Viral Load Trajectories Predict Cervical Cancer Risk in Prospective Study - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A prospective study in Changzhou, China, found that the trajectory of HPV viral load over time can predict cervical cancer risk. Women with persistently high viral loads had a 90.4% risk of developing cervical precancerous lesions, compared to 10.9% in the low stable group. This study improves clinical risk stratification by incorporating longitudinal viral trends, offering personalized follow-up protocols.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Saturday, November 28, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Cancer HPV Viral Load Trajectories Predict Cervical Cancer Risk in Prospective Study by November 23, 2026 in Cancer, Health Reading Time: 3 mins read 0 HPV Viral Load Trajectories Predict Cervical Cancer Risk in Prospective Study Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram In a newly published prospective cohort study, researchers report that longitudinal trends in human papillomavirus (HPV) viral load can serve as an informative indicator of cervical cancer risk among women with persistent infections. The findings, published online in CANCER, a peer-reviewed journal of the American Cancer Society, indicate that women exhibiting increasing or persistently high viral-load patterns face a substantially elevated likelihood of developing cervical precancerous lesions compared to those with stable or declining levels. By tracking these viral dynamics over time, clinical teams may gain crucial diagnostic insights that extend well beyond the baseline measurements collected during an initial medical evaluation. While persistent infection with high-risk human papillomavirus strains has long been recognized as central to the pathogenesis of cervical malignancies, longitudinal changes in viral concentration have remained largely unexamined in clinical research. To address this gap, an investigative team led by researchers at the Changzhou Maternal and Child Health Care Hospital of the Changzhou Medical Center at Nanjing Medical University in China prospectively tracked a cohort of 624 women diagnosed with persistent HPV infections, systematically analyzing their viral measurements across time. Through statistical modeling of the prospective data, the researchers identified four distinct trajectories of viral load among the study participants: low stable, moderate declining, moderate increasing, and persistently high. Evaluating outcomes across these four discrete categories revealed stark disparities in the frequency of cervical abnormalities. Specifically, cervical precancerous lesions developed in 10.9 percent of women within the low stable group and in 17.8 percent of those categorized in the moderate declining group. In sharp contrast, cervical precancerous lesions were identified in 74.2 percent of women in the moderate increasing trajectory and reached 90.4 percent among women exhibiting persistently high viral loads. Discover more Find Cancer Care See an OB-GYN cancer CANCER cancers When comparing relative disease risk across the cohorts, the investigators determined that trajectory patterns were associated with dramatic elevations in the probability of lesion development. Using the low stable trajectory group as the clinical baseline reference, women characterized by a moderate declining viral load exhibited a 1.95-fold higher risk of developing cervical precancerous lesions. For patients with upward or consistently elevated trajectories, the risk escalated sharply, with the moderate increasing group experiencing 29.66 times higher risk and the persistently high trajectory group experiencing an extraordinary 124.29-fold increase in the risk of precancerous lesions relative to the low stable group. Importantly, incorporating longitudinal viral trends directly enhanced clinical risk stratification models. The researchers found that evaluating trajectory patterns substantially improved clinicians’ ability to forecast an individual patient’s risk of developing cervical lesions compared to reliance on conventional predictors alone. While baseline clinical characteristics and initial viral load provide useful starting data, accounting for the direction and trajectory of viral measurements over subsequent visits provided measurable added value in estimating long-term clinical outcomes. The study’s authors emphasized that viral dynamics clarify why individual outcomes can diverge widely among patients who appear clinically comparable upon initial examination. Co-corresponding author Lina Zhang, MD, noted that two women presenting with similar HPV viral loads during a single clinical appointment may ultimately experience very different health trajectories depending entirely on how those concentrations evolve over time. Addressing the broader management of persistent human papillomavirus infections, co-corresponding author Zhonghua Shi, MD, highlighted the clinical imperative to balance vigilance with intervention restraint. A primary obstacle in managing persistent infections has been distinguishing women who genuinely require intensified clinical surveillance from those who can safely avoid redundant or invasive examinations. Shi and colleagues expressed hope that mapping viral trajectories will inform more personalized follow-up protocols, directing intensive monitoring to high-risk individuals while refining prevention strategies across cervical healthcare. Discover more Cancer Check Symptoms Book Lab Tests Book a Checkup Try Science Kits The prospective analysis, published online by Wiley in CANCER, reflects the journal’s interdisciplinary mission as an American Cancer Society publication dedicated to integrating worldwide oncologic research on the etiology, course, and treatment of human cancers. HPV Detected in 91.6% of Respiratory Papillomatosis Patients - Bioengineer.org

Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Saturday, November 28, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health HPV Detected in 91.6% of Respiratory Papillomatosis Patients by November 23, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 5 mins read 0 HPV Detected in 91.6% of Respiratory Papillomatosis Patients Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram A comprehensive systematic review and meta-analysis published in the Journal of Infection and Public Health has quantified the global prevalence of human papillomavirus (HPV) in patients with recurrent respiratory papillomatosis (RRP). The study, led by Ahmad Adebayo Irekeola and colleagues, synthesized data from 66 distinct studies conducted across diverse geographical and clinical settings. The central finding indicates that HPV was detectable in 91.6% of the 2,214 patients with RRP included in the analysis, establishing a high baseline prevalence of the virus in this rare chronic disorder. This quantitative synthesis addresses a previous gap in the epidemiological literature, providing a standardized measure of HPV detection that complements existing evidence on genotype distribution and disease burden. Recurrent respiratory papillomatosis is a rare condition characterized by the development of recurrent papillomatous lesions throughout the respiratory tract, with the larynx being the most frequently affected site. Although histologically benign in most cases, the disease can cause substantial morbidity through progressive voice impairment, airway obstruction, and the necessity for repeated surgical interventions. The condition exhibits a bimodal distribution, comprising juvenile-onset RRP (JoRRP) and adult-onset RRP (AoRRP). JoRRP generally presents during childhood and is associated with perinatal acquisition of HPV, whereas AoRRP typically develops in adulthood and is thought to arise from sexual transmission via oral-genital contact. Despite its rarity, RRP imposes a disproportionate clinical burden on patients, families, and healthcare systems due to the need for prolonged and repeated procedures. The pathogenesis of RRP is overwhelmingly associated with low-risk HPV genotypes, particularly types 6 and 11. While other genotypes, including oncogenic types, have occasionally been detected, the predominance of HPV6 and HPV11 distinguishes RRP from many other HPV-associated diseases where high-risk genotypes like HPV16 and HPV18 drive malignant transformation. Understanding the prevalence of HPV infection provides an epidemiological framework for interpreting differences in genotype distribution and clinical presentation. However, previous studies have generally been derived from individual, often small, institution-based populations, providing limited insight into the overall global prevalence of HPV in RRP patients. Discover more Explore Data Book Care Services Find Treatments Find Cancer Care Study Biology To address this gap, the researchers conducted a broad electronic literature search in PubMed, Web of Science, Scopus, and ScienceDirect from database inception to March 16, 2026. The search strategy combined terms relating to both the disease and the virus, including “human papillomavirus,” “recurrent respiratory papillomatosis,” and specific subtypes. After screening 3,097 records and removing duplicates, 66 studies met the eligibility criteria for inclusion. These studies were published between 1987 and 2025, with study periods spanning from 1971 to 2021. The majority of the included studies were retrospective in design, representing diverse geographical settings across Africa, Asia, Europe, North America, Oceania, and South America. The meta-analysis employed a random-effects model based on the DerSimonian–Laird method to calculate pooled estimates, anticipating considerable clinical, geographical, and methodological heterogeneity. The primary outcome was the prevalence of HPV in patients with RRP, defined as the proportion of patients who tested positive for HPV among those assessed. Secondary outcomes included the prevalence of specific HPV genotypes, particularly HPV6 and HPV11. The methodological quality of the included studies was assessed using the Joanna Briggs Institute Critical Appraisal Checklist for Studies Reporting Prevalence Data. Given a minimum overall score of 77.8%, the studies were classified as having a low risk of bias, ensuring a robust foundation for the quantitative synthesis. The pooled prevalence of HPV among all patients with RRP, irrespective of subtype, was 91.6% (95% CI: 88.2–94.2). Considerable between-study heterogeneity was observed, with an I2 statistic of 76.2%. To assess the robustness of this estimate, the researchers performed leave-one-out sensitivity analyses, which showed that the pooled prevalence ranged only from 91.4% to 92.1% following the sequential exclusion of individual studies. This stability indicates that no single study substantially altered the overall pooled estimate, reinforcing the consistency of HPV detection across the included populations. Subgroup analyses revealed variations in HPV prevalence across different geographical regions and study periods. The highest prevalence was observed in North America at 94.2%, followed by Oceania at 93.2% and South America at 92.6%. The lowest pooled prevalence was found in Asia at 86.6%. When stratified by study period, studies conducted from 2015 onwards showed a higher pooled prevalence of 94.6% compared to 88.5% for studies conducted before 2015. The authors caution that this temporal difference may reflect methodological variations or differences in population characteristics rather than a genuine increase in HPV prevalence, as diagnostic methods and study designs varied across the included literature. Discover more Read Med Journals Find ENT Doctors Find Lung Care Book a Checkup Check Symptoms Genotype-specific analyses demonstrated substantial differences in the distribution of HPV6 and HPV11. HPV6 was detected in 61.1% of patients across 52 studies, whereas HPV11 was detected in 41.2% across 49 studies. These genotypes also showed different patterns according to RRP subtype. In patients with JoRRP, the pooled prevalence of any HPV was 94.7%, with HPV6 detected in 58.6% and HPV11 in 50.2%. In contrast, among patients with AoRRP, the pooled prevalence of any HPV was 86.5%, with HPV6 detected in 68.1% and HPV11 in 32.9%. The higher prevalence of HPV11 in JoRRP is consistent with previous observations linking this genotype to younger age at diagnosis and potentially more aggressive disease characteristics. The study also examined the impact of specimen type on HPV detection rates. Studies using invasive specimens, such as tissue biopsies, demonstrated a pooled HPV prevalence of 92.2%. In comparison, studies using non-invasive or minimally invasive specimens, such as saliva or oral rinses, showed a lower pooled prevalence of 82.9%. This difference is methodologically plausible, as tissue obtained directly from papillomatous lesions provides the most direct evidence of HPV infection within the disease site. HPV detected in oral fluids may vary according to disease activity and viral shedding, and cannot invariably be assumed to originate from the RRP lesion itself. Assessment of publication bias using funnel plots and Egger’s regression test indicated significant asymmetry, suggesting the possibility of small-study effects. Following the application of the trim-and-fill procedure, the adjusted pooled prevalence was 87.1% (95% CI: 82.7–90.6). While this adjustment accounts for potentially missing studies, the authors note that this method relies on assumptions that may not fully account for the substantial heterogeneity inherent to prevalence meta-analyses. Therefore, the adjusted estimate should be interpreted as a sensitivity measure rather than a definitive correction of the primary finding. The study has several strengths, including the incorporation of evidence from 66 studies spanning several decades and multiple geographical regions. The stability of the overall estimate in sensitivity analyses strengthens confidence that the principal finding was not driven by a single influential study. However, limitations include the limited number of studies in some geographical and methodological subgroups, which restricts the precision of their corresponding estimates. Additionally, individual-level factors such as vaccination history, disease duration, and immune status could not be consistently examined due to the reliance on aggregate published data. The findings reinforce the central epidemiological role of HPV in RRP and provide a global quantitative benchmark for future epidemiological and vaccine-impact studies. The observed high prevalence across diverse settings supports the interpretation of RRP as a predominantly HPV-associated disease across the lifespan. Continued surveillance using incident RRP cases, individual vaccination histories, and standardized HPV detection methods will be critical for determining whether increasing vaccination coverage translates into sustained reductions in HPV-associated RRP and for clarifying the longer-term epidemiological impact of prevention programs. Subject of Research: Global prevalence of human papillomavirus in patients with recurrent respiratory papillomatosis: A systematic review and meta-analysis Article Title: Global prevalence of human papillomavirus in patients with recurrent respiratory papillomatosis: A systematic review and meta-analysis Article References: Irekeola, A. ( Pexel ) Summary India has vaccinated over 10 million 14-year-old girls against HPV in less than nine months. Mint explains the campaign’s progress, the science behind it and the challenges ahead. This is a Mint Premium article gifted to you. Subscribe to enjoy similar stories. India has administered more than 10 million doses of the human papillomavirus (HPV) vaccine to 14-year-old girls in less than nine months under a campaign by the Union health ministry. The drive has covered nearly 87% of the annual target cohort of about 12 million girls, drawing praise from the World Health Organization’s (WHO) South-East Asia Region.

Original source text (English)
Explainer Mint explainer: why HPV vaccination is critical for girls as India crosses 10 million doses Priyanka Sharma 4 min read 27 Nov 2026, 01:31 PM IST India has delivered more than 10 million HPV vaccine doses to 14-year-old girls in under nine months, reaching nearly 87% of its target group. ( Pexel ) Summary India has vaccinated over 10 million 14-year-old girls against HPV in less than nine months. Mint explains the campaign’s progress, the science behind it and the challenges ahead. This is a Mint Premium article gifted to you. Subscribe to enjoy similar stories. India has administered more than 10 million doses of the human papillomavirus (HPV) vaccine to 14-year-old girls in less than nine months under a campaign by the Union health ministry. The drive has covered nearly 87% of the annual target cohort of about 12 million girls, drawing praise from the World Health Organization’s (WHO) South-East Asia Region. The milestone comes against a stark public-health backdrop. According to estimates by the WHO’s cancer agency, India records 1,27,526 new cervical cancer cases and 79,906 deaths every year, making it the second most common cancer among Indian women after breast cancer. The rapid vaccination marks a shift from treating cervical cancer at later stages to preventing it at the source. But the campaign’s long-term impact will depend on sustained coverage, awareness and access to screening and treatment. Mint explains why the vaccination drive matters, how it works and how states are faring. Also Read | Why India’s HPV vaccination drive is a shot in the arm to end cervical cancer Why is HPV vaccination critical for girls in India? India reports more new cervical cancer cases and deaths than any other country, accounting for one-fifth of global cases and nearly a quarter of all cervical cancer deaths worldwide. According to GLOBOCAN 2022 estimates, India records 1,27,526 new cases and 79,906 deaths annually, making cervical cancer the second most common cancer among Indian women after breast cancer. Because persistent infection with high-risk HPV strains—which are linked to over 80% of cervical cancer cases in India—drives the vast majority of the disease, immunising girls offers direct, long-term protection against a largely preventable cancer. Which vaccine is being used, and why is it given at age 14? The national campaign uses Gardasil-4, a quadrivalent recombinant vaccine manufactured by MSD (Merck), which protects against high-risk, cancer-causing HPV strains. The vaccine is procured with support from Gavi, the Vaccine Alliance. The shot is administered at age 14 because the vaccine works best when given well before any potential exposure to the virus. Nearly two decades of scientific research and real-world experience, backed by more than 800 million doses administered globally, show strong, lasting protection against vaccine-targeted HPV strains and precancerous lesions. Meanwhile, Serum Institute of India’s indigenous quadrivalent vaccine, CERVAVAC , received WHO prequalification in September 2026, expanding future domestic supply options for public programmes and UN agencies. What is the policy framework behind the rollout? India’s campaign aligns with the WHO’s global 90–70–90 strategy to eliminate cervical cancer by 2030. The strategy calls for vaccinating 90% of girls by age 15, screening 70% of women by ages 35 and 45, and treating 90% of women diagnosed with cervical disease. Following recommendations from the National Technical Advisory Group on Immunisation (NTAGI) and the Union government’s push in the Interim Budget 2024–25, Prime Minister Narendra Modi launched the nationwide campaign in February 2026. Also Read | India to launch nationwide HPV vaccination drive to eradicate cervical cancer India adopted a single-dose regimen, endorsed by the WHO as offering comparable protection to multi-dose schedules in young adolescents. The approach lowers procurement costs, simplifies cold-chain logistics and removes the risk of dropouts between doses. After an intensive 90-day campaign phase, the shot is being integrated into Routine Immunisation days at Ayushman Arogya Mandirs, primary health centres (PHCs), community health centres (CHCs) and district hospitals. Why can’t India rely on cancer screening alone? Screening through Visual Inspection with Acetic Acid (VIA), Pap smears or HPV-DNA tests can detect precancerous changes early. But national screening uptake among Indian women aged 30–49 remains around 1.9%, far below global benchmarks. Low awareness, social stigma and weak follow-up for confirmation and treatment mean that roughly 60% to 70% of cervical cancer cases in India are detected only at advanced stages. Vaccinating adolescents interrupts the chain of infection upfront, reducing the future burden of disease and the need for expensive, late-stage cancer care. However, vaccination does not replace screening, which remains important for women who are already eligible. India crossing the milestone of 10 million HPV vaccine doses is a significant step towards strengthening preventive healthcare for women. It reflects the government’s commendable commitment to protecting women’s health from an early age. HPV vaccination is one of the most effective interventions for preventing cervical cancer, said said Dr Archana Dhawan Bajaj, gynaecologist and fertility expert at Nurture Clinic. Ideally, girls should receive the vaccine between 9 and 14 years of age, preferably before exposure to HPV. Catch-up vaccination is recommended up to 26 years, while women aged 27–45 may consider it after medical consultation. Vaccination, complemented by regular cervical cancer screening, can significantly reduce India’s cervical cancer burden and save lives, she added. How are states managing the rollout, safety and digital tracking? Under Centre-State coordination, six states—Gujarat, Uttar Pradesh, Andhra Pradesh, Mizoram, Madhya Pradesh and Bihar—have achieved 100% coverage of their identified target populations, while 12 states have crossed 80%. Uttar Pradesh leads in volume, with more than 29.33 lakh girls vaccinated, followed by Bihar with over 13.70 lakh and Gujarat with over 7.75 lakh. Uptake has also been strong in Assam (95.63%), Telangana (95.28%), Karnataka (93.16%) and Uttarakhand (90.17%). State-led drives in Bihar, Tamil Nadu and Sikkim have been folded into the national mission. Also Read | HPV vaccine: Is one dose enough for girls? Should males get this vaccine? All sessions are free and voluntary, require parental consent and are conducted under trained medical officers, with links to 24×7 facilities to manage any rare adverse events following immunisation (AEFI). Specialists from AIIMS, alongside teachers, ASHAs and ANMs, lead grassroots awareness efforts. The U-WIN digital platform enables slot booking, digital certificates and real-time district-level tracking of coverage gaps. 研究发现,将 HPV 疫苗接种 扩展到危地马拉男孩具有成本效益 - Bioengineer.org

Expanding HPV Vaccination to Guatemalan Boys Is Cost-Effective, Study Finds - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报Bioengineer.org 于 2026 年 11 月 26 日发表的一项研究发现,将 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 疫苗接种 扩大到危地马拉的男孩具有经济效率。这项研究由 Beatriz Hernández 领导,利用本地数据评估了将男性 队列研究 纳入 HPV 免疫计划的长期成本效益。该研究比较了两种策略:只为女孩接种疫苗,每避免一个残疾调整生命年 (DALY) 的增量成本效益比 (ICER) 估计为 1,089 美元;对女孩和男孩都接种疫苗,每避免一个 DALY 的 ICER 为 1,959 美元。这两种策略均在可接受的成本效益阈值之内,因为其成本仍低于危地马拉人均 GDP 基准 4,354.89 美元。该分析使用 PLANTEVEC 静态模型并遵循 CHEERS 2022 指南。研究结果表明,将 疫苗接种 扩大到男孩对于公共卫生部门来说是一种经济上合理的方法,支持以公平为导向的预防工作。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于 2026 年 11 月 26 日发布。该分析评估了 100 年范围内的长期经济成果,发布日期为最新参考日期。
地点与对象
该研究以危地马拉为重点,专门检查了公共卫生系统及其人口,重点关注 10 岁女孩以及可能将男孩纳入 HPV 疫苗接种 计划的情况。
事件
Beatriz Hernández 领导的研究人员进行的一项研究发现,根据使用当地​​流行病学和成本数据进行的成本效益分析,将 HPV 疫苗接种 扩大到危地马拉的男孩具有经济效率。Bioengineer.org 于 2026 年 11 月 26 日报道了该研究。
规模、影响与风险
该研究比较了两种 疫苗接种 策略:一种仅针对女孩,预计可避免每个 DALY 的 ICER 1,089 美元;另一种针对女孩和男孩,预计可避免每个 DALY 的 ICER 1,959 美元。这两种策略均在可接受的成本效益阈值之内,因为其成本仍低于人均 GDP 基准 4,354.89 美元。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究得出的结论是,将 HPV 疫苗接种 覆盖范围扩大到男孩对于危地马拉的公共卫生部门来说在经济上是合理的。该分析基于 PLANTEVEC 静态模型并遵循 CHEERS 2022 指南。研究结果基于建模数据,并不代表已确认的 暴发 或实时公共卫生反应。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Expanding HPV Vaccination to Guatemalan Boys Is Cost-Effective, Study Finds - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A study published on November 26, 2026, by Bioengineer.org found that expanding human papillomavirus (HPV) vaccination to boys in Guatemala is economically efficient. The research, led by Beatriz Hernández, evaluated the long-term cost-effectiveness of including male cohorts in HPV immunization programs using locally derived data. The study compared two strategies: vaccinating only girls, which had an estimated incremental cost-effectiveness ratio (ICER) of USD 1,089 per disability-adjusted life year (DALY) avoided, and vaccinating both girls and boys, with an ICER of USD 1,959 per DALY avoided. Both strategies were found to be within the accepted cost-effectiveness thresholds, as their costs remained below Guatemala’s GDP per capita benchmark of USD 4,354.89. The analysis used the PLANTEVEC static model and followed the CHEERS 2022 guidelines. The findings suggest that extending vaccination to boys is an economically justifiable approach for the public health sector, supporting equity-oriented prevention efforts.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Saturday, November 28, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Expanding HPV Vaccination to Guatemalan Boys Is Cost-Effective, Study Finds by November 26, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 2 mins read 0 Expanding HPV Vaccination to Guatemalan Boys Is Cost-Effective, Study Finds Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram A long-term cost-effectiveness analysis evaluated the economic efficiency of expanding human papillomavirus vaccination to boys within Guatemala’s public health system. Cervical cancer remains a leading cause of cancer-related morbidity and mortality across Guatemala, resulting in 1,555 new diagnosed cases and 872 deaths each year. On a global level, cervical cancer represents a substantial preventable malignancy burden that disproportionately affects low- and middle-income nations. Although Guatemala has provided human papillomavirus vaccination to ten-year-old girls since 2018, public health decision-makers have faced the critical policy question of whether extending routine immunization coverage to boys represents an efficient allocation of limited resources. To address this question, researchers led by Beatriz Hernández evaluated the long-term economic outcomes of extending vaccination programs using locally derived epidemiological and cost data. The investigation adopted the perspective of the national public health system and implemented the PLANTEVEC static model. Methodological design and reporting strictly adhered to the CHEERS 2022 guidelines. To capture long-term health and financial impacts, the investigators applied an extensive 100-year analytic horizon. Future health outcomes and economic costs were both discounted at a standard annual rate of 3 percent, ensuring consistency across long-range projections. Model estimates established incremental cost-effectiveness ratios, or ICERs, measured in United States dollars per disability-adjusted life year avoided. The evaluated interventions were compared directly against Guatemala’s gross domestic product per capita, which was established at USD 4,354.89 as the decision benchmark. Maintaining vaccination exclusively for girls yielded an estimated ICER of USD 1,089 per DALY avoided. In comparison, transitioning to a gender-neutral vaccination strategy covering both girls and boys yielded an estimated ICER of USD 1,959 per DALY avoided. Crucially, the modeled costs of both immunization strategies remained well below the benchmark threshold of one GDP per capita. Based on these findings, the study concluded that human papillomavirus vaccination strategies in Guatemala are economically efficient under established decision criteria. While expanding routine immunization to include boys carries a higher cost per disability-adjusted life year avoided than vaccinating girls alone, the gender-neutral intervention remains fully within accepted cost-effectiveness thresholds. Consequently, extending coverage beyond ten-year-old girls to include male cohorts provides an economically justifiable approach for the public health sector that directly supports equity-oriented prevention across the country. 魁北克省逐步推出 HPV 自检以进行 宫颈癌 筛查

Quebec gradually rolls out HPV self-testing for cervical cancer screening
新闻简报魁北克省正在逐步引入用于 宫颈癌 筛查的 HPV 自检,旨在为那些在传统盆腔检查方面面临障碍的个人提供更多机会。该计划目前正在Chaudière-Appalaches地区推出,允许符合条件的个人使用拭子和运输管收集自己的样本,然后将其送往医疗诊所或测试中心。卫生部和魁北克省卫生局已要求全省的医院和地区明年提供自我采样服务。然而,由于需要对魁北克使用的不同 HPV 检测进行实验室验证,因此实施是渐进的。医学微生物学家 Dumaresq 指出,大约四分之一的女性没有及时进行 宫颈癌 筛查,而自我收集选项旨在弥补这一差距。阳性结果需要进一步测试,例如盆腔检查或阴道镜检查,以确定是否存在癌性或癌前病变。该计划由 RAMQ 承保,虽然尚未确定全省范围内的推广日期,但妇科肿瘤学家 Salvador 希望该计划能够在 2027 年初至中期在整个魁北克推广。该举措是通过 疫苗接种 和筛查来改善早期检测和预防 宫颈癌 的更广泛努力的一部分。
查看新闻五要素
时间
魁北克省逐步推出用于 宫颈癌 筛查的 HPV 自检,该计划目前正在 Chaudière-Appalaches 实施,预计将于 2027 年初至中期在全省范围内实施。该计划于2026年11月27日公布。
地点与对象
魁北克省,特别是 Chaudière-Appalaches 地区,以及更广泛的魁北克女性群体,她们可能因不适、焦虑或缺乏医疗保健而推迟了 宫颈癌 筛查。
事件
魁北克省正在引入 HPV 自检来进行 宫颈癌 筛查,作为传统盆腔检查的替代方案,该计划正在全省逐步推广。Santé Québec 和卫生部证实了这一点,并在 Chaudière-Appalaches 首次实施。
规模、影响与风险
该计划旨在增加 宫颈癌 筛查的机会,特别是对于那些在传统考试中面临障碍的个人。预计这将使很大一部分人口受益,杜马雷斯克估计,大约四分之一的女性没有及时接受筛查。该计划不直接报告病例或死亡情况,但强调了对早期发现和生存率的潜在影响。
应对、状态与不确定性
该计划正处于实施阶段,每个地区都需要验证实验室测试。Santé Québec 已开始按照更新的指南与卫生机构合作,使用不同测试的地区必须完成自己的验证。该服务由 RAMQ 覆盖,积极的结果需要进一步测试。该举措预计将于 2027 年初至中期在全省范围内推广。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Quebec gradually rolls out HPV self-testing for cervical cancer screening

News Brief
Quebec is gradually introducing HPV self-testing for cervical cancer screening, aiming to increase access for individuals who face barriers to traditional pelvic exams. The initiative, currently being rolled out in the Chaudière-Appalaches region, allows eligible individuals to collect their own samples using a swab and transport tube, which are then sent to medical clinics or test centers. The Health Ministry and Santé Québec have requested that hospitals and regions across the province make self-sampling available next year. However, implementation is gradual due to the need for laboratory validation of different HPV tests used in Quebec. Dumaresq, a medical microbiologist, noted that about one in four women is not up to date with cervical cancer screening, and the self-collection option is intended to address this gap. Positive results require further testing, such as a pelvic exam or colposcopy, to determine if cancerous or precancerous changes are present. The program is covered by RAMQ, and while no provincewide rollout date has been confirmed, Salvador, a gynecologic oncologist, hopes it will be available across Quebec by early to mid-2027. The initiative is part of broader efforts to improve early detection and prevent cervical cancer through vaccination and screening.
Original source text (English)
Skip to main content Montreal Calgary Edmonton Halifax Kitchener Montreal Ottawa Toronto Vancouver Winnipeg About About Us Our Team Contact Contact Us Advertise With Us Submit a News Tip News All Local Canada World Watch All Latest Videos Weather Montreal All Montreal Calgary Edmonton Halifax Kitchener Montreal Ottawa Toronto Vancouver Winnipeg About All About Us Our Team Contact All Contact Us Advertise With Us Submit a News Tip Quebec gradually rolls out HPV self-testing for cervical cancer screening A swab and collection tube used by patients to collect their own samples for human papillomavirus (HPV) testing as part of cervical cancer screening. (Courtesy: Santé Québec Chaudière-Appalaches) By News Staff Posted November 27, 2026 7:31 pm. Quebec is gradually introducing self-collection for cervical cancer screening, offering an alternative to traditional pelvic exams for people who may have delayed testing or face barriers to accessing health care. The initiative is currently being rolled out in Chaudière-Appalaches. Dr. Jeannot Dumaresq, a medical microbiologist and infectious diseases specialist with Santé Québec in the region, says the Health Ministry and Santé Québec have asked hospitals and regions across the province to make self-sampling available next year. The tests detect human papillomavirus (HPV), a virus that can cause cervical cancer, and allow eligible individuals to collect their own samples without requiring a health-care professional to perform the initial pelvic exam. A swab and collection tube used by patients to collect their own samples for human papillomavirus (HPV) testing as part of cervical cancer screening. (Courtesy: Santé Québec Chaudière-Appalaches) Dr. Shannon Salvador, a gynecologic oncologist at the Jewish General Hospital and an associate professor at McGill University, says the initiative could help reach people who have difficulty accessing screening. “This is an incredible progression for Quebec and being able to treat and screen people who could potentially have cervix cancer or pre cancer of the cervix,” Salvador said. Removing barriers to screening Dumaresq says the self-collection option is intended to reach people who may be uncomfortable with traditional screening or have put it off for years. “A lot of people are reluctant to have a pelvic exam for the cervical cancer screening either because of anxiety, because of discomfort, past trauma or sometimes cultural norms,” he said. He estimates that roughly one in four women is not up to date with cervical cancer screening. Dumaresq says cervical cancer is one of the most preventable cancers, thanks to vaccination and screening. He says early detection is associated with very high survival rates, while the chances of survival drop sharply when the disease is diagnosed at a later stage. Salvador says the test could make screening more accessible to people who struggle to reach health-care providers. “The individuals who can benefit the most from this test are those who do not have easy access to the healthcare system, whether they don’t have a family doctor or they’re too far remote and they can’t come into an area where there is healthcare professionals who do this test,” she said. How self-collection works The process involves a swab and a transport tube. Eligible individuals can collect their own sample at a clinic or take the kit home before returning it to a health-care provider. Dumaresq explains how the sample is collected. “We give the person a kit. There’s a swab, there’s a transport tube and then the person has to insert the swab just a few centimeters inside the vagina, just turns it there for a couple of seconds, then we put it back in the transport tube and that’s it. And then we bring it back to the medical clinic, the local service point or the test center,” he said. The service is covered by the Régie de l’assurance maladie du Québec (RAMQ). For now, there are no plans to distribute the kits by mail. Dumaresq says they are available through medical clinics known as local service points. People with a prescription can also obtain a kit at traditional testing centres, complete the sample at home or on-site and return it to their health-care provider. Positive results require further testing A positive HPV result does not mean someone has cervical cancer, but further investigation is necessary. Dumaresq says anyone who tests positive will need further screening to determine whether cancerous or precancerous changes are present. “We have to make sure that the person knows that if the result is negative, that we will just do another screening in a couple of years. But if the test is positive, the person has to know that then a pelvic exam will be needed to collect some cells directly in the cervix to look at the cells and see if there is cancerous or pre-cancerous cells,” he said. Salvador says people with positive results should be followed in a specialized clinic, such as a colposcopy clinic, where they can be assessed by a gynecologist or gynecologic oncologist. She says laboratory validation has also helped address concerns about the reliability of samples collected by patients themselves. “They have now shown that with the new validations that they do in the lab, they’re able to identify that the sample is just as good as a sample collected within a clinic by a healthcare professional,” Salvador said. If a sample does not meet laboratory validation requirements, Salvador says the person will be notified and may be asked to repeat the test or have a health-care professional collect the sample. Provincewide rollout expected next year Although Santé Québec and the Health Ministry have asked regions to offer self-sampling next year, implementation will be gradual because laboratories use different HPV tests, and each must be validated to ensure it works with self-collected samples. Dumaresq says the validation process is necessary because the tests used in Quebec laboratories vary. “In Canada there is no HPV or human papuomavirus test that is approved by Health Canada for self-sampling. So every lab had to do what we call a validation to make sure that the kits, the sampling kits we use, the tests we use in the lab are compatible and it works well with self-sampling,” he said. Dumaresq says regions using the same laboratory test as Chaudière-Appalaches can use the results of its validation work, while regions using different tests must complete their own validation. In a statement to CityNews, Santé Québec said it began working with health and social services institutions following a September 2026 update to HPV self-sampling guidelines and standards by the Institut national d’excellence en santé et en services sociaux (INESSS). “The next steps will be determined once this work with the network is completed,” the statement said. “Each region will be able to organize implementation based on its local context and available resources.” Santé Québec also confirmed that its Chaudière-Appalaches institution is currently the only one gradually rolling out self-sampling for cervical cancer screening. Salvador hopes the program will be available across Quebec by early to mid-2027, although a provincewide implementation date has not been confirmed. She stresses that timely follow-up is essential for anyone who receives a positive result. “If you know that you are positive, is it extremely important that you get in for the next steps, which is a colposcopy. To miss that is the shame of why did we even do the test in the first place,” she said. In the longer term, Dumaresq hopes self-collection will help detect more precancerous changes before they develop into cancer, potentially preventing cases and deaths. Time
Quebec gradually rolls out HPV self-testing for cervical cancer screening, with the initiative currently being implemented in Chaudière-Appalaches and expected to be provincewide by early to mid-2027. The program was announced on November 27, 2026.
Location and population
Quebec, specifically the Chaudière-Appalaches region, and the broader population of Quebec women who may have delayed cervical cancer screening due to discomfort, anxiety, or lack of access to healthcare.
Event
Quebec is introducing HPV self-testing for cervical cancer screening as an alternative to traditional pelvic exams, with the initiative being rolled out gradually across the province. This is confirmed by Santé Québec and the Health Ministry, with the first implementation in Chaudière-Appalaches.
Scale, impact and risk
The initiative aims to increase access to cervical cancer screening, particularly for individuals who face barriers to traditional exams. It is expected to benefit a significant portion of the population, with Dumaresq estimating that roughly one in four women is not up to date with screening. The program does not directly report cases or deaths but highlights the potential impact on early detection and survival rates.
Response, status and uncertainty
The program is in the implementation phase, with validation of laboratory tests required for each region. Santé Québec has begun working with health institutions following updated guidelines, and regions using different tests must complete their own validation. The service is covered by RAMQ, and positive results require further testing. The initiative is expected to be provincewide by early to mid-2027.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Review Maps How Papillomavirus and Tumor Barriers Disrupt Natural Killer Immunity - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A review published in Frontiers in Cellular and Infection Microbiology details how high-risk human papillomavirus (HPV) infection disrupts natural killer (NK) cell immunity in cervical cancer. HPV16 and HPV18 are responsible for approximately 70% of cervical cancer diagnoses globally. The review highlights that HPV infection leads to a suppressive ecosystem that impairs NK cell recruitment, spatial penetration, receptor activation, and cytotoxicity. While the review synthesizes translational and preclinical evidence, it does not provide definitive treatment or prevention strategies.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Saturday, November 28, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Review Maps How Papillomavirus and Tumor Barriers Disrupt Natural Killer Immunity by November 24, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 6 mins read 0 Review Maps How Papillomavirus and Tumor Barriers Disrupt Natural Killer Immunity Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram In a comprehensive narrative review published in Frontiers in Cellular and Infection Microbiology, researchers led by Bowen Xu and colleagues synthesized translational and preclinical evidence detailing how high-risk human papillomavirus (HPV) infection coordinates multilayered tumor immune microenvironment barriers to drive natural killer (NK) cell dysfunction in cervical cancer. Cervical cancer remains a significant global health burden as the fourth most common cancer in women, accounting for approximately 570,000 new cases and 311,000 deaths annually worldwide, with high-risk HPV types 16 and 18 responsible for roughly 70 percent of all diagnoses. The authors emphasize that although NK cells act as critical innate effectors capable of recognizing and destroying transformed cells in an antigen-independent manner, their antitumor efficacy within cervical tumors is progressively blunted. Rather than stemming purely from isolated intrinsic cellular defects, this functional decline reflects an integrated, actively maintained suppressive ecosystem that impedes NK cell recruitment, spatial penetration, receptor activation, and cytotoxicity. The investigators conducted a structured literature search across PubMed/MEDLINE, Web of Science, and Scopus, focusing predominantly on studies published over the past five years alongside foundational landmark mechanistic research. To establish rigorous interpretation across diverse experimental systems, the review explicitly categorized evidence into three distinct tiers: cervical cancer-specific evidence obtained directly from patient tissues or cell models, HPV-associated mechanistic findings detailing viral oncoprotein effects, and cross-tumor mechanistic evidence derived from other solid tumors or general preclinical systems. The authors noted that cross-tumor findings serve only to provide biological plausibility and mechanistic context rather than proof of dominant drivers in cervical malignancy. Furthermore, because the evaluation was structured as a narrative synthesis rather than a quantitative systematic review or meta-analysis, formal risk-of-bias scoring was omitted, though potential publication biases, model heterogeneity, and the translational limits of extrapolating preclinical findings to human disease were critically integrated into their evaluations. At the molecular initiator level, persistent high-risk HPV infection actively sabotages innate immune perception through the coordinated action of its viral oncoproteins. Experimental studies highlighted in the review demonstrate that HPV18 E7 functions as a branch-selective modulator of the cGAS–STING cytosolic DNA-sensing pathway; rather than causing broad pathway inhibition, E7 selectively suppresses STING-induced NF-κB signaling by restricting the nuclear accumulation of p65, while leaving the parallel STING–TBK1–IRF3 signaling cascade relatively preserved. Concurrently, HPV E7 impairs inflammatory signaling by binding IFI16 and recruiting the E3 ubiquitin ligase TRIM21, driving the ubiquitination and degradation of IFI16-associated inflammasome complexes and drastically reducing the production of interleukin-18, an essential cytokine required for NK cell activation and proliferation. Additionally, HPV16 E5 alters Golgi trafficking and chaperone interactions to selectively downregulate surface expression of classical human leukocyte antigen molecules HLA-A and HLA-B, blunting CD8+ T-cell recognition while deliberately preserving inhibitory ligands that suppress NK cells. Discover more Study Biology cancers cancer Find Cancer Care Cancer Beyond viral oncoprotein signaling, physical and structural remodeling of the extracellular matrix establishes a rigid mechanical barrier that physically excludes NK cells from the tumor core. The synthesis details how cancer-associated fibroblasts drive excessive deposition of collagen fibers, fibronectin, and hyaluronic acid, altering tissue mechanical properties. Studies indicate that when the pore size of dense collagen networks contracts below 2 square micrometers, cytotoxic lymphocyte migration is completely blocked, forcing lymphocytes to switch from efficient ameboid movement to slow, inefficient interstitial crawling that reduces tissue patrolling speed. In vitro and engineered-matrix models show that while fibronectin stabilizes immune synapses between T cells and tumor cells, it disrupts synaptic stability in NK cells, prompting continuous migration and diminished killing capacity. Although multiplex immunohistochemistry of human cervical cancer samples confirms that NK cells and CD8+ T cells are restricted largely to the surrounding stroma rather than tumor epithelial nests, specific matrix features driving this spatial exclusion in patients require further characterization. Cellular components of the tumor immune microenvironment further amplify this immune-restricted state through interlocking suppressive networks. Regulatory T cells directly curb NK cell activation, cytokine production, and degranulation through competitive consumption of interleukin-2 and secretion of transforming growth factor-beta and interleukin-10. Tumor-associated neutrophils release reactive oxygen species, arginase, and proteases that damage surface activating receptors, while tumor-associated macrophages secrete vascular endothelial growth factor and prostaglandins to enforce immunosuppression, though much of this detailed myeloid-suppression data originates from non-cervical tumor models. Notably, the review highlights that cervical cancer tissue analyses have identified a spatially associated subpopulation of LAMP3-positive dendritic cells that express indoleamine 2,3-dioxygenase 1. This enzyme accelerates tryptophan depletion and kynurenine accumulation, activating aryl hydrocarbon receptor pathways to foster regulatory T-cell tolerance. However, the authors caution that existing cervical evidence for LAMP3-positive dendritic-cell interactions through IL-15 receptor or NECTIN2–TIGIT signaling remains correlative and lacks direct causal confirmation. Impaired NK cell trafficking and effector fitness are also driven by dysregulated chemokine signaling and severe metabolic constraints within the cervical tumor milieu. Chemotactic axes critical for NK cell recruitment, including CCL5-CCR5, CCL27-CCR10, and CX3CL1-CX3CR1, are frequently restricted by microenvironmental signals, while hypoxic conditions downregulate CCR1, CCR5, and CXCR3 expression on NK cells. Furthermore, disruptions to the interferon-gamma–CXCL9/CXCL10–CXCR3 recruitment circuit hamper lymphocyte infiltration into tumors. Concurrently, metabolic stressors including lactate accumulation, extracellular acidification, adenosine–A2A receptor signaling, and nutrient deprivation compromise NK cell cytotoxicity, although their specific contributions in human cervical cancer await direct characterization. Clinical investigations in HPV-associated cervical lesions document marked downregulation of key activating receptors—including NKG2D, NKp30, and NKp46—alongside reduced perforin levels and increased inhibitory signaling via non-classical HLA-E binding to CD94/NKG2A and elevated TIGIT expression on CD56-bright NK subsets, tilting the receptor balance toward profound functional inactivation. The review examines how current clinical immunotherapies intersect with these microenvironmental hurdles, pointing out that established treatments do not automatically reverse NK cell impairment. While the KEYNOTE-A18 trial validated the clinical benefit of adding the PD-1 inhibitor pembrolizumab to chemoradiotherapy in patients with newly diagnosed, high-risk locally advanced cervical cancer, and cemiplimab demonstrated survival improvements in recurrent or metastatic disease, these achievements do not demonstrate restored NK cell cytotoxicity, as neither trial verified NK-specific functional recovery. Conversely, direct clinical evaluation of the anti-NKG2A antibody monalizumab in advanced gynecologic cancers, including cervical carcinoma under trial NCT02459301, yielded no objective tumor responses despite good tolerability. Furthermore, an expansion cohort combining monalizumab with durvalumab in trial NCT02671435 reported an objective response rate of zero percent in cervical cancer patients. These clinical outcomes demonstrate that simply blocking inhibitory checkpoints on NK cells is insufficient without overcoming concurrent microenvironmental constraints. To address these limitations, emerging NK cell-directed therapies are being explored, though they remain largely confined to preclinical testing in cervical oncology. Adoptive transfer using allogeneic umbilical cord blood-derived or stem-cell-derived NK cells provides HLA-independent tumor killing, yet clinical translation is hampered by limited in vivo persistence, host-mediated clearance, cytokine dependence, and rapid suppression upon entering the tumor microenvironment. Chimeric antigen receptor engineering offers enhanced selectivity; specifically, anti-FAP CAR-NK cells have demonstrated preclinical efficacy against FAP-expressing cervical cancer cells and cancer-associated fibroblasts in three-dimensional spheroid models, while anti-mesothelin CAR-NK cells show activity against mesothelin-expressing targets. However, the authors stress that target heterogeneity and the risk of off-tumor stromal toxicity remain major concerns, particularly given the functional diversity of fibroblasts. Cytokine therapies utilizing IL-15 superagonists or memory-like NK cells pre-activated with IL-12 and IL-15 show promise in enhancing cytotoxicity, but their clinical utility must balance antitumor potency against systemic toxicities and lack of durability. The review synthesizes clinicopathological and prognostic studies evaluating NK cell parameters, concluding that NK cell functional phenotype provides far greater predictive insight than raw cell abundance. In a retrospective clinical cohort of 101 postoperative cervical cancer patients receiving adjuvant radiotherapy by Wang and colleagues, circulating NK cell proportions below 16 percent or absolute counts below 200 cells per microliter correlated with poorer overall survival in univariable analyses, though statistical significance was lost after multivariable adjustment. In a tissue-microarray cohort of 406 patients evaluated by Zou and colleagues, intratumoral CD57-positive cell density declined with advancing clinical stage, contributing to a composite immune score distinguishing 3-year survival rates of 96.6 percent versus 83.0 percent, though CD57 is not entirely NK-specific. Additionally, single-cell transcriptomic analyses by Liu and colleagues linked mature cytotoxic CD16-positive NK cell signatures to longer survival, whereas Gutierrez-Silerio and Cao reported progressive receptor loss and exhaustion profiles across advanced disease stages. Looking toward future therapeutic design, the authors propose rational, barrier-oriented combination strategies that integrate extracellular matrix remodeling, cytokine metabolic support, and targeted checkpoint inhibition. For example, combining matrix-degrading enzymes or anti-FAP CAR-NK cells with adoptive cell transfer could mitigate physical exclusion, while pairing TGF-beta pathway inhibitors with PD-1/PD-L1 blockade could relieve dual immunosuppressive mechanisms. Similarly, cytokine priming might compensate for HPV-driven IL-18 deficiency. Nonetheless, the authors caution that these combinatorial concepts remain hypothesis-generating and carry significant safety risks, including excessive immune activation, severe inflammatory toxicities, and impaired normal tissue repair. The synthesis concludes that clinical progress demands prospective validation of standardized spatial and cellular biomarkers, rigorous stratification by HPV infection status, and carefully designed trials evaluating whether multi-target regimens can safely dismantle the tumor microenvironment barrier and restore durable natural killer cell immunity in cervical cancer. 🇿🇦 83 percent fewer girls in South Africa carry the most dangerous HPV types - www.theangryoptimist.org

🇿🇦 83 percent fewer girls in South Africa carry the most dangerous HPV types - www.theangryoptimist.org
新闻简报《柳叶刀全球健康》的一项研究显示,南非17岁和18岁女孩中高危型HPV 16和18型的患病率已从2019年的21.6%显着下降到2023年的3.2%。在感染艾滋病毒的女孩中,这一数字从29.4%下降到4.4%。疫苗接种 计划于 2014 年开始,在公立学校免费提供 HPV 疫苗,该计划非常有效,甚至将未接种疫苗的女孩中的 HPV 患病率 减少了 74%。这是首次在人口层面出现这种减少。
查看新闻五要素
时间
Photo: ©epicurean via Canva.com The share of 17- and 18-year-old girls with HPV types 16 and 18 fell from 21.6 percent in 2019 to 3.2 percent in 2023.
地点与对象
南非,17 岁和 18 岁的女孩,包括感染艾滋病毒的女孩。
事件
在接种疫苗和未接种疫苗的女孩中,高危型HPV 16 型和 18 型的 患病率 显着降低,总体降低了 83%。
规模、影响与风险
在接种疫苗的女孩中,HPV 16 和 18 型的 患病率 从 2019 年的 21.6% 下降到 2023 年的 3.2%,在感染艾滋病毒的女孩中从 29.4% 下降到 4.4%。该研究涉及来自南非四个省份的 2,357 名女孩。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究由 Wits RHI 和柯比研究所的研究人员进行,研究结果发表在《柳叶刀全球健康》杂志上。该计划已在减少 HPV 患病率 方面取得了成功,但进一步的研究正在进行中。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

🇿🇦 83 percent fewer girls in South Africa carry the most dangerous HPV types - www.theangryoptimist.org

News Brief
A study in The Lancet Global Health shows that the prevalence of high-risk HPV types 16 and 18 among 17- and 18-year-old girls in South Africa has significantly decreased from 21.6% in 2019 to 3.2% in 2023. Among girls with HIV, the decrease was from 29.4% to 4.4%. The vaccination program, which began in 2014 and provides free HPV vaccines in public schools, has been highly effective, even reducing HPV prevalence among unvaccinated girls by 74%. This is the first time such a reduction has been shown at the population level.
Original source text (English)
Photo: ©epicurean via Canva.com The share of 17- and 18-year-old girls with HPV types 16 and 18 fell from 21.6 percent in 2019 to 3.2 percent in 2023. Among girls with HIV, the share fell from 29.4 to 4.4 percent. Even girls who had not been vaccinated had 74 percent lower prevalence of these types, because the virus spreads less when most people are vaccinated. HPV, human papillomavirus, is a virus that spreads through sex. Some types of the virus can lead to cervical cancer. Types 16 and 18 cause about 70 percent of all cases worldwide. South Africa began vaccinating against HPV in 2014. The vaccine is given free of charge in public schools to girls in Grade 4 who are at least nine years old. It protects against types 16 and 18. In the first year, 87 percent of the girls received at least one dose. From one in five to three in a hundred Researchers from Wits RHI at the University of the Witwatersrand and the Kirby Institute at the University of New South Wales have studied how the program works. The results are published in a study in The Lancet Global Health. Cervical cancer often takes decades to develop. So the researchers measured how common the virus is, since it is the virus that causes the cancer. The study is based on 2,357 girls aged 17 and 18. They came from 15 health clinics in four provinces: Free State, Gauteng, Mpumalanga and North West. The girls took a sample themselves, which was tested for 28 HPV types. The first group was examined in 2019. Those girls were too old to have been offered the vaccine. Among them, 21.6 percent carried type 16 or 18. The second group was examined in 2023 and had been offered the vaccine. In that group, the share was 3.2 percent. That is a reduction of 83 percent. Among the girls whose vaccination was on record, the share was 1.5 percent. That is 92 percent lower than before the program. The same effect for girls with HIV About a quarter of the girls were living with HIV. In South Africa, about 7.9 million people were living with HIV in 2025, or 12.3 percent of the population. HIV makes it more likely that HPV stays in the body and leads to cancer. Among girls with HIV, the share with type 16 or 18 fell from 29.4 to 4.4 percent. That is a reduction of 82 percent. For girls without HIV, the result was about the same. According to Wits, this is the first time in the world that this has been shown at the population level. The protection also reaches the unvaccinated Girls who had not been vaccinated also had less HPV in 2023. Among them, 5.5 percent carried type 16 or 18, which is 74 percent lower than in 2019. The researchers attribute this to herd immunity. When most people are vaccinated, the virus spreads less, and that also protects those who have not received the vaccine. Types 31 and 45, which are related to 16 and 18, also declined. The share fell from 16.6 to 5.7 percent. Other HPV types did not decline. According to the researchers, this strongly suggests that the vaccine, and not changes in sexual behavior, is behind the decline. Two doses were enough South Africa gave two doses. Even so, the decline was as large as in wealthy countries that gave three doses. The researchers say that part of the explanation is that more than 80 percent of the girls were vaccinated in the first years. In 2022, the World Health Organization, WHO, recommended that one dose is enough for most people. For people with HIV, WHO still recommends three doses. South Africa switched to one dose in 2024. We have previously written that cervical cancer could become the first type of cancer to be completely eliminated. Every year, more than 660,000 women worldwide get cervical cancer. More than 90 percent of the cases and deaths occur in low- and middle-income countries. According to Gavi, South Africa has one of the world's highest burdens of the disease. Photo: ©epicurean via Canva.com The share of 17- and 18-year-old girls with HPV types 16 and 18 fell from 21.6 percent in 2019 to 3.2 percent in 2023.
Location and population
South Africa, 17- and 18-year-old girls, including those with HIV.
Event
A significant reduction in the prevalence of high-risk HPV types 16 and 18 among vaccinated and unvaccinated girls, with a 83% decrease overall.
Scale, impact and risk
The prevalence of HPV types 16 and 18 fell from 21.6% in 2019 to 3.2% in 2023 among vaccinated girls, and from 29.4% to 4.4% among girls with HIV. The study involved 2,357 girls from four provinces in South Africa.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study was conducted by researchers from Wits RHI and the Kirby Institute, and the results are published in The Lancet Global Health. The program has shown success in reducing HPV prevalence, but further investigation is ongoing.

“畅读会员”点此登录查看原文链接 Review Open access ## 摘要### 研究背景高危型人乳头瘤病毒(HPV)的持续性感染是宫颈癌及多种其他上皮恶性肿瘤的重要病因学基础。因此,深入剖析HPV早期的进入过程和细胞内转运,对于寻找阻断HPV感染的新靶点至关重要。### 主要内容HPV的进入并非由单一受体介导的线性过程,而是一个跨越基底膜(BM)/细胞外基质(ECM)、质膜、内体、高尔基体相关区室以及细胞核的多步骤转运网络。在研究相对充分的HPV16及假病毒(PsV)模型中,HPV病毒粒子可被与BM/ECM相关及位于细胞表面的附着因子捕获并发生初始附着,其中包括层粘连蛋白-332(LN332)和硫酸乙酰肝素蛋白聚糖(HSPGs)。随后,宿主因子如亲环蛋白B(CyPB)和弗林蛋白酶参与衣壳构象预激活、L2氨基端暴露以及弗林蛋白酶介导的L2切割,从而为病毒粒子从初始附着过渡至进入平台的组装创造条件。整合素/黏着斑激酶(FAK)信号传导、富四跨膜蛋白微区(TEMs)、膜联蛋白A2/S100A10异四聚体(A2t)以及细胞骨架相关调控因子,均被认为参与进入平台的组织、非典型内吞和早期细胞内转运;但其作用环节及依赖程度可能因HPV型别、细胞模型和实验体系而异。病毒粒子进入内体系统后,γ-分泌酶介导的L2膜插入、Retromer/Retriever介导的逆行转运,以及由囊泡包裹且依赖有丝分裂的核内进入过程,被认为有助于将L2/病毒DNA(vDNA)复合物递送至细胞核。### 简短结论本综述总结了HPV进入过程中涉及的关键宿主因子、膜平台、非典型内吞途径、内体分选事件、逆行转运通路及核内进入过程,并讨论了它们作为HPV感染早期干预靶点的潜在价值。## 摘要### 研究背景高危型人乳头瘤病毒(HPV)的持续性感染是宫颈癌及多种其他上皮恶性肿瘤的重要病因学基础。因此,深入剖析HPV早期的进入过程和细胞内转运,对于寻找阻断HPV感染的新靶点至关重要。### 主要内容HPV的进入并非由单一受体介导的线性过程,而是一个跨越基底膜(BM)/细胞外基质(ECM)、质膜、内体、高尔基体相关区室以及细胞核的多步骤转运网络。在研究相对充分的HPV16及假病毒(PsV)模型中,HPV病毒粒子可被与BM/ECM相关及位于细胞表面的附着因子捕获并发生初始附着,其中包括层粘连蛋白-332(LN332)和硫酸乙酰肝素蛋白聚糖(HSPGs)。随后,宿主因子如亲环蛋白B(CyPB)和弗林蛋白酶参与衣壳构象预激活、L2氨基端暴露以及弗林蛋白酶介导的L2切割,从而为病毒粒子从初始附着过渡至进入平台的组装创造条件。整合素/黏着斑激酶(FAK)信号传导、富四跨膜蛋白微区(TEMs)、膜联蛋白A2/S100A10异四聚体(A2t)以及细胞骨架相关调控因子,均被认为参与进入平台的组织、非典型内吞和早期细胞内转运;但其作用环节及依赖程度可能因HPV型别、细胞模型和实验体系而异。病毒粒子进入内体系统后,γ-分泌酶介导的L2膜插入、Retromer/Retriever介导的逆行转运,以及由囊泡包裹且依赖有丝分裂的核内进入过程,被认为有助于将L2/病毒DNA(vDNA)复合物递送至细胞核。### 简短结论本综述总结了HPV进入过程中涉及的关键宿主因子、膜平台、非典型内吞途径、内体分选事件、逆行转运通路及核内进入过程,并讨论了它们作为HPV感染早期干预靶点的潜在价值。 打赏 展开全文 开通VIP 享海量文章 全站开放获取+解锁超过 200万篇新闻全文+每日更新 2000+篇,学术前沿动态全时追踪! 推荐 观点 时政 湖北 武汉 世相 环球 专题 极客圈 经济 城建 科教 健康 悠游 相亲 汽车 房产 消费 创意 文化 体育 娱乐 影像 帅作文 International 职教院 酒道 登录 | 极目新闻客户端 推荐 观点 时政 湖北 武汉 世相 环球 专题 极客圈 经济 城建 科教 健康 悠游 相亲 汽车 房产 消费 创意 文化 体育 娱乐 影像 帅作文 International 职教院 酒道 湖北过半适龄女性接种HPV疫苗 湖北日报 2026-11-26 21:26:07 阅读量: 湖北日报全媒记者余瑾毅 11月25日,大学生小王(化姓)在湖北省妇幼保健院完成了首剂进口九价HPV疫苗接种。HPV疫苗纳入国家免疫规划一年来,我省逐步形成免费接种保障基础、惠民减免回应多元需求的接种模式,覆盖适龄女孩并向更广泛人群延伸。截至11月24日,全省HPV疫苗首剂累计接种217483剂次,全省HPV疫苗首剂接种覆盖率67.39%。 接种疫苗的女孩 我国宫颈癌发病率呈上升趋势,防控关口在于“早” 近年来,我国宫颈癌发病率呈上升趋势,宫颈癌发病年龄呈现年轻化趋势。湖北省妇幼保健院妇科首席专家、湖北省宫颈癌防治中心主任吴绪峰介绍,HPV即人乳头瘤病毒,超过99%的宫颈癌都和高危型HPV的持续感染相关。在中国,常见的感染型别为HPV16、52、58三种。 HPV的主要传播途径是通过皮肤、黏膜或体液直接接触传播,其中性接触是最常见的传播方式。“需要说明的是,并非只有发生性行为才会感染,只要存在皮肤黏膜的直接接触,理论上就存在感染风险。” 幸运的是,宫颈癌也是一种可以预防的癌症,有成熟的三级预防体系。吴绪峰说,一级预防包括健康教育+HPV疫苗接种,接种疫苗是核心手段。二级预防指适龄女性定期筛查+癌前病变规范管理。三级预防是宫颈癌确诊后的规范治疗与康复,属于筛查失守后的补救手段。 “需要提醒的是,很多人认为身体没有不适,就不需要做筛查。现实中,从未参加筛查的女性,一旦出现出血症状再来就诊,往往已是局部晚期宫颈癌。”吴绪峰说。 2025年11月10日起,我国将HPV疫苗正式纳入国家免疫规划,为2011年11月10日以后出生、年满13周岁的女孩免费接种二价HPV疫苗,全程2剂次。 武汉市疾病预防控制中心免疫规划所副主任医师许慧琼表示,这能显著降低未来宫颈癌及其癌前病变的发病率,也能有效缩小城乡、区域和群体间的健康差距。同时大家也可以根据自身需求,自费选择九价HPV疫苗。 惠民减免覆盖更广人群,“男女共防”很重要 目前,除免疫规划疫苗免费为适龄女孩提供外,还有多种非免疫规划HPV疫苗供受种者自愿、自费接种。我省多地在落实国家免疫规划基础上,还通过地方惠民活动丰富了接种选择。 许慧琼详细介绍,武汉推出了进口九价HPV疫苗的限时优惠接种活动,覆盖了9-14岁的女孩,还有18—26岁的女性和男性,给家长和孩子多一个选择。 由中华医学会妇科肿瘤学分会等七个学会共同制定发布的《预防性人乳头瘤病毒疫苗中国临床应用指南(2025版)》,优先推荐9—26岁女性接种HPV疫苗,其中9—14岁女孩是重点人群;同时推荐27—45岁女性和9—26岁男性接种。 据悉,HPV感染并非女性“专属”,HPV感染对男女均有明确健康危害。高危型HPV感染引发阴茎癌、肛门癌、口咽癌。 “值得注意的是,很多男性感染后没有明显症状,属于无症状携带者,容易在不知情的情况下传染给伴侣。”吴绪峰说,HPV防控是全人群的公共卫生问题。 HPV感染可防可控,关键在于早接种早预防 在武汉市汉兴街第二社区卫生服务中心,负责人王星翘频繁遇到家长关于HPV疫苗的疑问。他说,很多人把HPV等同于性病,忽略了提前接种、尽早保护才是更有效的预防方式。其次,部分家长忽视了为男孩接种的重要性。 针对家长普遍关心的疫苗安全性问题,王星翘明确表示,HPV疫苗属于重组蛋白疫苗,只含有病毒外壳的蛋白质颗粒,不含病毒DNA,不含激素,不含任何影响生殖功能或生长发育的成分。全球数亿剂次的接种实践表明,没有证据显示接种HPV疫苗会影响生长发育或干扰内分泌系统。 世界卫生组织明确推荐,9-14岁未发生性行为的女孩是首要接种人群。9-14岁女孩接种后产生的抗体水平是15岁以上人群的2倍以上,保护效果可持续至少10年,仅需接种两剂即可获得长期保护。 王星翘表示,2011年11月10日以后出生、年满13周岁的女孩可免费接种二价HPV疫苗,能预防约70%的宫颈癌。对于希望获得更广泛保护的家庭,9-14岁女性及18-26岁男性和女性,知情自愿自费接种进口九价HPV疫苗,可享受6.7折优惠价格。他总结,HPV感染可防可控,关键在于“早接种、早预防、不恐慌、不拖延”。 责任编辑:张扬 相关新闻 极目新闻
